# Parity notes — Epi Info Web Data Entry

This file is the single ledger of the Epi Info web data-entry migration: every reconciled assumption, every departure from legacy Epi Info Enter, every parity gap and every undelivered item, together with the run report, which console output never replaces. Each status is `pass`, `fail` or `not run`. Only results produced by a real run count as verified; readings of the seed scripts and the legacy source are labelled "static finding (planning time)".

## Run results

This section is the run report of the final-tree run of 2026-11-26 on the run tree ("Run"), whose results hold for the tree of the commit that delivers this report, except where "Changed after the run" names a change merged after the run and the checks run for it. The success criteria, the database precondition, the checks that need no database and the build and restore results below come from that run, and every other subsection names the tree it was checked on. Earlier results, the final-tree runs of the 13:11Z run's tree and of `d450f367` among them, are history under "Earlier runs".

Merged after the run as well: the exact-number and number-entry change, in `src/EpiInfo.Domain/Values/FieldValue.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Domain/Values/FieldValueCoercion.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Api/Json/FieldValueJsonConverter.cs`, `web/src/api/client.ts`, `web/src/api/types.ts`, `web/src/state/fieldEventQueue.ts` and `web/src/components/fields/NumberInput.tsx`, with its tests in `tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/CheckCode/FieldEventEndpointTests.cs`, `tests/EpiInfo.Domain.Tests/Validation/RecordValidatorTests.cs`, `tests/EpiInfo.CheckCode.Tests/Evaluation/EvaluatorTests.cs`, `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.test.ts`, `web/src/state/fieldEventQueue.test.ts` and `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`: a number whose nearest double is whole but which is not an exact 64-bit integer keeps its exact JSON number token in `FieldValue`, and the API writes that token, so an `ASSIGN` that copies 1.0000000000000001 answers 1.0000000000000001 instead of `1`, and saving it on an integer column is the field-level 400 `The number is not a whole number within the range this field can store.` with nothing written; the client reads such a token as an exact decimal and recognises an exact decimal only when it made it, not by its shape, so a `values` map whose text fields are named `kind` and `literal` is sent as an object instead of a bare number; the field-event queue checks assigned values with the client's own field-value check, so an assigned 9007199254740993 is applied with the rest of the answer instead of discarding it; and on leaving a number field, text that stands for the field's value, such as `1e2` or `045`, is shown as that number (100, 45), with no change to the draft. Merged after the run as well: the client response-handling change, in `web/src/api/client.ts`, `web/src/api/errors.ts` and `web/src/api/types.ts`, with its tests in `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.test.ts` and `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`: a `DELETE` completes only on 204, and any other 2xx is a server failure that offers Retry; a response whose body cannot be read or parsed keeps its status, so a 2xx is a server failure and a 503 the database-unavailable failure, each with `writeOutcome` `null`, so a failed save offers only Save again, and only a rejected `fetch` is a network failure; a form definition whose option values, options lists or bounds do not match the published shape for their field type is refused; `GET /health` counts as healthy only on 200 with `status` `healthy`; `ApiError` gains `type`, read only from a ProblemDetails body whose `status` equals the HTTP status; and `FieldEventKind` and `WriteOutcome` alias the generated types. Merged after the run as well: the connection-pool recovery change, in `src/EpiInfo.Data/Sql/SqlConnectionFactory.cs`, with its tests in `tests/EpiInfo.Data.Tests/Sql/SqlErrorClassifierTests.cs`, which extend `SqlErrorClassifierTests` and add the classes `SqlConnectionFactoryConnectionStringTests` and `SqlConnectionFactoryRecoveryTests`: every connection opens with `Pool Blocking Period=NeverBlock;` placed ahead of the configured connection string, so an unset Pool Blocking Period becomes `NeverBlock` and the next request and `/health` succeed as soon as SQL Server answers again, where SqlClient's default blocking period kept answering 503 for seconds after it returned; an explicit `Pool Blocking Period` in the configured string is honoured, a string that holds no setting passes unchanged, and during an outage each open now waits for SqlClient's own connect attempt, up to `Connect Timeout`, before it answers 503. Merged after the run as well: the save-stamp and write-port change, in `src/EpiInfo.Api/Services/SaveStampProvider.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Domain/Storage/IDataStore.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Data/Records/SqlRecordRepository.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Data/SqlServerDataStore.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Api/Services/RecordService.cs` and `tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/Infrastructure/FakeDataStore.cs`, and this file's "Logon stamp format (A11)" paragraph, with its tests in `tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/Errors/ErrorContractTests.cs` (`StoreFailureWriteOutcomeTests`), `tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/Persistence/StoredDataContractTests.cs` (`SqlRecordRepositoryWriteTests` and the new classes `FakeDataStoreStoredNumberTests` and `StoredDataContractSoftDeleteLogTests`), `tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/Records/ListAndSearchTests.cs`, `tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/Records/PermissionTests.cs`, `tests/EpiInfo.Data.Tests/Records/IdentifierProvenanceTests.cs` and `tests/EpiInfo.Data.Tests/Records/ReservedWordIdentifierTests.cs`: an API process whose OS user name is blank no longer stamps `unknown`, because the stamp provider refuses it; `IDataStore.UpdateAsync` and `SoftDeleteAsync` take a stamp factory, which the store calls at most once, inside the transaction, only after it has decided to write, so such a process refuses an insert, an update or a soft delete of an active record as a 500 `notWritten` with nothing written, while a 404, 409, 400 or 403 and the 204 of an already-deleted record keep their answers; a soft delete returns a `SoftDeleteResult` that carries the stored `GlobalRecordId`, which the post-commit log line names instead of the id as the route spelled it; and the test store keeps a value on a `float` column as the binary64 double SQL Server stores. Merged after the run as well: the results-table change, in `web/src/styles.css`, `web/src/components/RecordGrid.tsx`, `web/src/components/FindRecordDialog.tsx` and `web/src/components/PageTabs.tsx`, with its tests in `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`: the record grid and the Find results table use fixed table layout with 7rem starting columns, a long value ends in an ellipsis inside its cell and every non-blank cell carries its full value as `title`, and an open button's name and text stop at 150 code points followed by "…"; each table's scroll box is a focusable named region ("Record list", "Search results"); the current grid row is marked by a 4 px inset bar instead of a border, has a hover tint of its own (`--color-accent-subtle-hover`), and the grid's edges line up with its section; a blank first cell shows "Open", and shades mark grid columns scrolled out of view; and the page tabs reserve the width of their bold text, so selecting one moves nothing. Merged after the run as well: the toolbar-state and dirty-tracking change, in `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.ts`, `web/src/components/FormView.tsx`, `web/src/components/RecordNavigator.tsx`, `web/src/components/ConnectionError.tsx`, `web/src/App.tsx` and `web/src/styles.css`, with its tests in `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.test.ts`, `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx` and `web/src/App.test.tsx`: the toolbar status reads "Saving…" and then "Changes saved." after any successful save, the save made on leaving a record, Retry and Save again included, until the next edit, and a Back to forms that saved the draft shows "Changes to the <form> record were saved." on the forms list; while a dirty record's leave waits for its field events, "Saving…" shows and Save, Delete, the navigator and the grid are disabled; a field set back to its stored value leaves the dirty set unless a pending write carries it, so a revert sends no `PUT` and raises no leave prompt; Delete is disabled on an empty new record; and while an uncertain write blocks saving and leaving, the blocked controls are marked `aria-disabled` and still accept a press, each repeat of the banner is announced again, and Save again has a warning style. Merged after the run as well: the modal-dialog change, in `web/src/styles.css` and `web/src/components/MessageDialog.tsx`, with its tests in `web/src/components/FieldEffects.test.tsx`: while any modal dialog is open the page behind it is scroll-locked, and each dialog and its backdrop keep scrolling to themselves, so the Find, delete-confirmation and check-code message dialogs stay in place; and the check-code message counter reads "Message n of N" over the messages of the open session, advancing as each is dismissed ("1 of 3", "2 of 3", "3 of 3") and growing when messages arrive, where it read "Message 1 of N" over the messages still waiting and never advanced. Merged after the run as well: the failure-banner wording change, in `web/src/api/errors.ts`, `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.ts`, `web/src/components/ConnectionError.tsx` and `web/src/components/FormView.tsx`, with its tests in `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.test.ts` and `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`: a 413 on a save states the 1 MB limit, names the field whose text is largest and says the changes are still on screen; the 500 `notWritten` save banner says the changes are still on screen; the uncertain-write text exists once, shared by the editor and `ConnectionError`, and names its cause first; after Save again fails with `notWritten`, the banner shows the latest failure's cause and trace id and still offers only Save again; a 409 on a save says "This record has been deleted and is read-only. Your unsaved changes were not saved.", replaces the deleted-record status while shown, and moves focus to the form heading when focus is on the page body; and a save that fails while leaving a record adds "Leaving this record was cancelled.", followed for a 404, a 403 or a request-level 400 by "Your changes are still on screen.". Merged after the run as well: the grid search and paging chrome change, in `web/src/components/RecordGrid.tsx` and `web/src/styles.css`, with its tests in `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`: the grid's one status line, "Loading records…", "No records found." or the record count, with its Retry button, follows "Show deleted" in the controls row instead of the footer below the rows, so a search or a toggle shows its outcome beside the controls, and it keeps one width whatever its text, so it does not hop between rows; the footer is rendered only with the paging; "Earlier records" and "Later records" form one group (`.record-grid__paging-actions`), so they wrap as a pair and keep their order; the table's sideways scroll returns to its start when the form, the search, the deleted toggle or the page changes, while a reload of the same page keeps it; and while the search box or the "Show deleted" box has focus, a list that shrinks no longer moves that control on screen, because the grid keeps the height the window's scroll offset needs until the list next changes. Merged after the run as well: the field-event payload and failure-notice change, in `web/src/state/fieldEventQueue.ts`, `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.ts`, `web/src/components/FormView.tsx` and `web/src/styles.css`, with its tests in `web/src/state/fieldEventQueue.test.ts` and `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`: a field event sends each checkbox as the record's `POST` sends it, `true` when checked and `false` otherwise, read-only boxes included, so check code such as `Fever = (-)` sees an untouched box as unchecked; a value without its field type's wire shape, such as `abc` typed into a number field, is sent as `null` instead of failing that event and every later one with a 400, and Save still reports it; a failed event is kept per field and event kind, and the notice lists one message per failed field, in the order of its first failure, until an event of that kind on that field is answered and applied, so raising another event, another field's success and a save no longer clear it, and opening another record clears them all; and the list is held still while a pointer is pressed, so a click is not lost to the notices changing under it. Merged after the run as well: the client request-deadline and Accept-header change, in `web/src/api/client.ts`, with its tests in `web/src/state/useRecordEditor.test.ts`, `web/src/state/useRecordNavigation.test.ts` and `web/src/state/fieldEventQueue.test.ts`: every request and its whole response must complete within 60 s (`REQUEST_DEADLINE_MS`), twice the API's default 30 s database command timeout, or the call fails with no response and is never resent, so a stalled read offers Retry and a stalled create or update counts as possibly applied and offers only Save again, where the editor could wait on "Saving…" without end; a caller's abort ends the call at once whatever the transport does, and a call already aborted sends nothing; an error body is decoded once, from the bytes received, so a body that is not JSON cannot pass for JSON by losing a second byte order mark; and every request sends `Accept: application/json, application/problem+json`, with `Content-Type` still only on the four calls that send a body. each counted `UPDATE`, of a page's columns, of the base stamp or of a soft delete, ends with `SELECT @@ROWCOUNT` and the store decides on that count, not on the provider's affected-row count, which is -1 under `NOCOUNT ON`, so there a missing page row is still inserted and the update repeated instead of a 200 that lost the submitted values, and a statement on the locked record that changes no row or more than one rolls the write back; and values the caller's metadata accepts but the transaction's own metadata read refuses, such as a field made read-only in between, and an insert whose form that read no longer finds, are a 500 `notWritten` with nothing written instead of a 500 `unknown`. Besides the eight projects and three props files it reads by fixed path, the guard's token scan now reads every `.csproj`, `.props` and `.targets` file anywhere under `src/` and `tests/`, both walked whole by `FilesWithin`, with `bin/` and `obj/` pruned, a link failing the walk and the extension matched in any case, so a project at the top of `tests/` or in a folder of any name, and a nested `Directory.Build.props` or `Directory.Build.targets`, is scanned; `SolutionProjects_AreExactlyTheEightNewProjects` compares every `tests/**/*.csproj` with the four test projects; only the legacy `tests/Tests.csproj`, by its exact name, is set aside, and only where `tests/` and `Tests/` are one folder, as on a case-insensitive file system (`TestsFolderIsLegacyTestsFolder`); and relative paths turn only the platform's directory separator into `/`, and reads split on `/` only (`ReadRelativeFileWithin`), so a backslash in a file name is kept. Merged after the run as well: the OpenAPI script containment change, in `web/scripts/api-generate.mjs` and `web/scripts/check-api-fresh.mjs`. `assertInsideCheckout` in `api-generate.mjs`, and its synchronous twin, examine each directory between the checkout and a file with `lstat` and refuse a symbolic link, a junction or anything else that is not a directory, as well as a file outside the checkout, with a `CheckoutPathError`; with `createMissing`, a missing directory is created one level at a time. `npm run lint` compares a committed file only when its directories pass that check, and opens it without following a link where the platform defines `O_NOFOLLOW`; on Windows, which does not, it compares the path's `lstat` with the opened file's device and inode. A committed file that is a link, or is reached through a linked directory, is stale, with a line naming the reason before its `stale:` line; the stale and current messages are unchanged. `npm run api:generate` checks the targets' directories before staging each file, after staging, before the second rename and before each rollback step and each removal of a staged file. It backs up a committed file through a no-follow open of the very file it examined, gives the copy that file's permission bits and refuses a link. When a rollback cannot put the first file back, its message says that the staged copy is kept, with the remedy to move it back; that it is gone; or that it could not be examined, with the remedy to move it back only once every directory above it is a directory of the checkout again and it is checked to be a regular file. The generation lock stays in `src/EpiInfo.Api/obj`, which, with every directory above it, must be a directory of the checkout, because the lock serializes the writers of exactly that `obj/` and `bin/`: a linked `obj/` fails both scripts before any lock file is created or any `dotnet` step starts. Its directories are checked before each lock operation: each attempt, claim, read, record update and release. A link in the place of the lock or a claim is refused with the remedy to delete it; a record update is created only under a name that nothing holds, so a link there fails the update. The cleanup of leftovers beside the lock removes only claims and updates whose recorded processes have ended on this host, or whose record is unreadable and older than 30 s, and leaves the others, reporting a link in a leftover's place with the remedy to delete it. After an interruption, a lock or claim that cannot be released, and a lock kept because its recorded child still runs, are reported on stderr. Known limits: each check runs immediately before the operation it guards, but Node's `fs` has no directory-relative operations, so a directory replaced between the check and the operation by a concurrent writer of the checkout is not detected; the Windows path, without `O_NOFOLLOW`, was not run on Windows. It alters no other file, no build file and not the OpenAPI document. The HTTP listener's `server.host` or `preview.host` must be `127.0.0.1`, `::1` or `localhost`, which is resolved once and must resolve to loopback addresses only, and the listener then binds the address checked, the first one `localhost` resolved to, instead of the name, which Node would resolve again when it listens; a separate HMR listener, whose host Vite takes before the plugin runs, needs `server.hmr.host` set to the numeric `127.0.0.1` or `::1`. - **Date and times**: 2026-11-26, UTC. The first step ended at 14:32:04Z, `npm run e2e`, the last step of the clean-checkout sequence, at 14:42:25Z, and the parallelism diagnostic of SC-1 at 14:50:10Z.
- **Toolchain**: .NET SDK 10.0.115, selected by `global.json` (10.0.100, `latestPatch`); Node v22.23.5; npm 11.21.3; Docker 29.10.0 with Docker Compose v5.8.0; sqlcmd 18.7.0001.1.
- **SQL Server**: Microsoft SQL Server 2022 (RTM-CU28) (KB5125472) 16.0.4325.4, Developer Edition (64-bit) on Linux (Ubuntu 22.04.5 LTS): the `sqlserver` service of `testdata/docker-compose.yml`, image `mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server:2022-latest`. `EpiRehearsal` after `db-init`: compatibility level 160, collation `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`.
- **Commands name the solution** (C-1): `dotnet build EpiInfo.Web.sln` and `dotnet test EpiInfo.Web.sln`, because the root also holds `Epi Info 7.sln`. Reproduced at run time, 14:32:19Z: a bare `dotnet build` at the root of the fresh clone exited 1 with `MSBUILD : error MSB1011: Specify which project or solution file to use because this folder contains more than one project or solution file.`
- **Earlier reports**: the final-tree runs of 2026-11-26 on the 13:11Z run's tree and on `d450f367`, and the report of 2026-11-25 on tree `22ead6a7`, which held only build manifests, are history under "Earlier runs"; this run supersedes all three. The 13:11Z run's tree is an intermediate tree of 2026-11-26, not in the published history; outside this file it differs from `fa428507` only in `web/src/components/FormView.tsx` and `web/src/components/FormView.test.tsx`, which lack that commit's banner-action focus change and its tests, and in `web/src/state/useRecordNavigation.test.ts`, whose New case covers the first and last positions.

**Adaptations.** The validation host is shared with other checkouts, so the run departed from the documented commands in these points only:

- The Compose file ran under its own project name, with an uncommitted override file that only publishes the `sqlserver` service on a private loopback port instead of 1433, caps the server at 32 GB (`MSSQL_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB` 32768) and limits its container to 40 GB. The committed file sets no cap; the image, the `db-init` service and its `testdata/init.sh` load are the committed file's. Tests reached the server through `ConnectionStrings__EpiInfo` and `SQL_HOST`, with the rehearsal development value documented in `testdata/README.md`.
- The 32 GB cap carries over from the `d450f367` run, whose first attempt with a 4,096 MB cap was discarded when `SearchStatementBoundsTests.Search_PackedEqualityAndLikeOnGlobalRecordId_DecodesOnceWithinTheDefaultTimeout` could not get a 2,348,416 KB memory grant under that cap. This run used 32 GB from the start.
- The run's Compose project was taken down with `docker compose down -v` before the run, so `docker compose up` created the network and the server container from nothing, as on a fresh host. An earlier attempt on the run tree, against a server left running, where `docker compose up` found the container running and healthy and started nothing, was set aside for this run; its test counts and its single failure were the same.
- The API for SC-1 and SC-2 ran from the build output (`dotnet run --project src/EpiInfo.Api --no-build --no-launch-profile`) on `127.0.0.1` port 20340, set through `Kestrel__Endpoints__Http__Url`, serving the `web/dist` that `npm run build` had just produced. The smoke test used `E2E_BASE_URL` with that address instead of Playwright's own server, because port 5000 belongs to another checkout.
- The server ran the image defaults: max degree of parallelism 0 and cost threshold for parallelism 5.

**Sequence as run**, in order, each from the repository root unless named; times are when the step ended:

1. `git rev-parse HEAD` and the tool versions, 14:32:04Z.
2. In `testdata/`: `docker compose up -d --wait sqlserver`, exit 0 in 11 s, the network and the container created and the container healthy, 14:32:15Z.
3. `docker compose run --rm db-init`, exit 0 in 4 s, 14:32:19Z.
4. `testdata/sql/99_verify.sql` through `sqlcmd`, straight after: nine rows, each `ok = 1`, 14:32:19Z ("Database precondition").
5. The C-1 reproduction and the project-folder restore and builds of "Build and restore", then `git clean -xdf -- src tests` and `dotnet restore EpiInfo.Web.sln`, 14:32:19Z to 14:32:29Z.
6. `dotnet build EpiInfo.Web.sln`, exit 0 in 6 s, 14:32:35Z.
7. `dotnet test EpiInfo.Web.sln`, exit 1 in 167 s, 14:35:22Z (SC-1).
8. Further test runs before the web steps: the rerun `dotnet test EpiInfo.Web.sln --no-build` without a database reset, exit 1, 14:38:12Z (SC-1); `--filter "Category!=Integration"` against an unreachable server, exit 0, 14:38:37Z ("Checks that need no database"); `--filter "Category=Integration&Category!=Performance"`, exit 1, 14:41:34Z, with `EpiInfo.Data.Tests` 195 of 196 passed (the packed timeout of SC-1) and `EpiInfo.Api.Tests` 251 of 251; SC-11's `--filter Category=Performance`, exit 0, 14:41:42Z; `--list-tests`, exit 0, 14:41:44Z.
9. In `web/`: `npm ci`, exit 0, 14:41:47Z; `npm run lint`, exit 0, 14:41:58Z; `npm run build`, exit 0, 14:42:03Z; `npm test`, exit 0, 14:42:19Z.
10. The API started; `GET /health` returned 200 after 2 s, 14:42:20Z, followed by the HTTP checks of SC-1.
11. `npm run e2e`, exit 0, 14:42:25Z, then the SQL readback of SC-2; the API stopped at 14:42:27Z.

The OpenAPI staleness checks followed, from 14:42:41Z to 14:43:47Z, then the Docker check, whose image was built from a clone of the run tree and probed from 14:43:49Z to 14:45:45Z, with the later checks listed there ("Docker check"), and the parallelism diagnostic of SC-1 ran from 14:46:56Z to 14:50:10Z.

### Database precondition

- **Status**: pass. All nine named checks of `testdata/sql/99_verify.sql:4-12` returned exactly once, each with `ok = 1`, and no other row.
- **Target**: the run's Compose `sqlserver` service ("Run"), SQL Server 2022 RTM-CU28 (16.0.4325.4) Developer Edition on Linux. `99_verify.sql` ran against `EpiRehearsal` straight after `docker compose run --rm db-init` exited 0, at 2026-11-26T14:32:19Z, before any test touched it.
- `testdata/init.sh`, which `db-init` runs, prints these rows but exits 0 even when a row has `ok = 0`, so only the rows establish the precondition, never the script's exit status.

| `check_name` | `actual` | `expected` | `ok` |
|--------------|----------|------------|------|
| `metaViews` | 2 | 2 | 1 |
| `metaPages` | 3 | 3 | 1 |
| `metaFieldTypes` | 28 | 28 | 1 |
| `CaseReport records` | 1409 | 1409 | 1 |
| `FoodHistory records` | 609 | 609 | 1 |
| `CaseReport1 orphans` | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| `FoodHistory2 orphans` | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| `FoodHistory3 orphans` | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| `soft-deleted records` | 29 | 29 | 1 |

- **Compatibility level**: no compatibility level is required. Catalogue discovery (`MetadataQueries.Columns`) reads its table-name list with `OPENJSON` at database compatibility level 130 or higher, and with a fixed fallback decoder of the JSON string syntax below it; both paths return the same rows. Find's packed binding, which a search above 2,000 alternatives uses (Search semantics, SQL Server limits), is compatibility-neutral as well: it decodes its packed lists with `SUBSTRING`, `CONVERT` and `VALUES` tables, which every level evaluates alike, uses no `OPENJSON`, and carries plan hints that make every level plan the decoding alike. In a run of 2026-11-26 that predates `d450f367`, `SearchStatementBoundsTests` and `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` passed against a copy of the rehearsal database restored at level 100 as against one at 160. The nine `99_verify` checks do not cover the level, and `testdata/sql/00_create_database.sql` pins none, so the database takes the server's `model` level. Measured read-only on `EpiRehearsal` (`sys.databases.compatibility_level`), 2026-11-25: 160. Final run on the run tree, measured straight after `db-init`: 160. In that run `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` passed 56 of 56 and `SearchStatementBoundsTests` 20 of 21, its packed `GlobalRecordId` case timing out under the server's default parallelism settings (SC-1).

### SQL parse check

- **Status**: pass, 2026-11-26, on the run tree: SQL Server 2022, the product's server, accepts every batch the run executed and every complete statement the code builds or the tests run, and every text either parser rejects is accounted for below. ScriptDom rejects one statement the tests run, the `%%lockres%%` read, only because its grammar lacks that SQL Server virtual column. `testdata/tools/lint_sql.py` reads only the seed scripts, so this T-SQL was checked by two SQL Server 2022 parsers instead; nothing under `testdata/` was edited or run for it.
- **Earlier checks**: the check of the 13:11Z run's tree on 2026-11-26 passed, both parsers accepting its 542 executed batches and 154 of its 165 statement templates, the other 11 being 8 partial statements and 3 detector excerpts. The check of `d450f367` on 2026-11-26, before that one, passed, both parsers accepting its 592 executed batches and 148 of its 156 statement templates, the other 8 being partial statements.

### OpenAPI staleness checks

Run 2026-11-26 on the run tree, in `web/` of the run's fresh clone after `npm ci`, with .NET SDK 10.0.115, Node v22.23.5 and openapi-typescript 7.13.0. The checks ended between 14:42:41Z and 14:43:47Z; each row gives the time its check ended. Each of the eight builds of `src/EpiInfo.Api` that these checks ran reported 0 warnings and 0 errors, and `eslint .` printed nothing in each lint. `web/scripts/check-api-fresh.mjs` and `web/scripts/api-generate.mjs` changed since the 13:11Z run's tree: both now parse their arguments (`--help` or `-h` prints the usage; any other argument exits 2 before anything runs), and an interrupted run stops the processes it started, removes its temporary directory and reports the interruption instead of staleness. The check itself is unchanged: it regenerates the pair into a temporary directory, compares each file with the committed one byte for byte after turning CRLF into LF, prints `stale: <path>` for each stale file and then "OpenAPI document or generated client is stale; run npm run api:generate" with exit 1, and otherwise prints "OpenAPI document and generated client are current." with exit 0.

| Check | Expected | Status | Evidence |
|-------|----------|--------|----------|
| `npm run lint` on a clean tree | passes | pass | `src/*/bin` and `src/*/obj` deleted first, so the build restored the four `src/` projects again. Exit 0 in 13 s, 14:42:41Z: "OpenAPI document and generated client are current." |
| `npm run lint` on a warm tree | passes | pass | Rerun with no clean: exit 0 in 11 s, 14:42:52Z, with the same line |
| After editing only `src/EpiInfo.Api/openapi/EpiInfo.Api.json` | fails with the stale message | pass | One space appended; `git status --porcelain` listed only ` M src/EpiInfo.Api/openapi/EpiInfo.Api.json`. Exit 1 in 11 s, 14:43:03Z: `stale: src/EpiInfo.Api/openapi/EpiInfo.Api.json`, then "OpenAPI document or generated client is stale; run npm run api:generate" |
| `npm run api:generate` after that edit | rewrites the committed pair unchanged | pass | Generation, comparison and lint together exit 0 in 16 s, 14:43:19Z. `npm run api:generate` printed "wrote src/EpiInfo.Api/openapi/EpiInfo.Api.json" and "wrote web/src/api/schema.ts"; `git diff --quiet` on the pair then exit 0, so both files are byte-identical to the committed pair; `npm run lint` exit 0, "current" |
| After editing only `web/src/api/schema.ts` | fails with the stale message | pass | One space appended; `git status --porcelain` listed only ` M web/src/api/schema.ts`. Exit 1 in 11 s, 14:43:30Z: `stale: web/src/api/schema.ts`, then the stale message |
| `npm run api:generate` after that edit | rewrites the committed pair unchanged | pass | Generation, comparison and lint together exit 0 in 17 s, 14:43:47Z: both files written, `git diff --quiet` on the pair exit 0, byte-identical to the committed pair; `npm run lint` exit 0, "current" |

Afterwards, at 14:43:47Z, `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` in the fresh clone printed nothing.

In the same clone, before these checks, `npm run build` exited 0 at 14:42:03Z. Its `tsc --noEmit` step is clean, so the generated client compiles under `strict`, and `vite build` (vite 5.4.21) transformed 61 modules into `dist/index.html` (0.62 kB), `dist/assets/index-gKCQlDzc.css` (20.52 kB) and `dist/assets/index-CSagwDJR.js` (254.19 kB).

### Docker check

`src/EpiInfo.Api/Dockerfile` (P2) builds in three stages, with the repository root as context. `node:22` runs `npm ci` on the two manifests it copies by name, then `npm run build` with `web/src` bind-mounted read-only for that step alone. `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0.103` restores from the manifests it copies by name and publishes the API, because `global.json` (10.0.100, `latestPatch`) admits only the 10.0.1xx band and the `sdk:10.0` tag carries SDK 10.0.404; before the publish it bind-mounts `src/` and `.git/index` read-only and copies out only the `*.cs` files of the four projects and the two committed settings files, `appsettings.json` and `appsettings.Development.json`, that git's index tracks. `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:10.0` runs the API as the image's non-root user `app`, with `Web__ClientPath=/app/client` and `ASPNETCORE_HTTP_PORTS` set empty. As the file's header states, the build therefore needs BuildKit, for `RUN --mount`, and a git checkout as its context. Checked 2026-11-26 on the run tree with Docker 29.10.0, between 14:43Z and 15:10Z, against the run's rehearsal SQL Server (see "Database precondition"). The Dockerfile is unchanged since the 13:11Z run's tree. The connection string was supplied only at run time, through `ConnectionStrings__EpiInfo`, with the rehearsal development value documented in `testdata/README.md`.

The `node:22` stage ran `npm ci` (320 packages) and `npm run build` (`tsc --noEmit`, then `vite build` 5.4.21, 61 modules, writing `index-gKCQlDzc.css` and `index-CSagwDJR.js`, the bundle the run's own `npm run build` wrote); the `sdk:10.0.103` stage ran `dotnet restore` (3.9 s), the copy of the tracked sources out of the mounted index, and `dotnet publish -c Release`; the `aspnet:10.0` stage copied the publish output to `/app` and `web/dist` to `/app/client` |

The `node:22` stage completed (`npm ci`, 320 packages; `npm run build`, 61 modules, the same `index-gKCQlDzc.css` and `index-CSagwDJR.js`); the `sdk:10.0.103` stage's `dotnet restore src/EpiInfo.Api/EpiInfo.Api.csproj` stopped with `error NU1301: Unable to load the service index for source https://api.nuget.org/v3/index.json.` and `The HTTP request to 'GET https://api.nuget.org/v3/index.json' has timed out after 100000ms.`, for `EpiInfo.Data.csproj` after 10.09 min and for `EpiInfo.Api.csproj` after 20.18 min. This is the validation host's bridge network (see "Build network" below), not the Dockerfile: the identical build with `--network host` passes (first row) |

- **Build context**: a git checkout, as the Dockerfile's header requires. The repository root of a clone is one, so the build runs from there as the plan's `docker build -f src/EpiInfo.Api/Dockerfile -t epiinfo-web .` does, adding `--network host` on this host (below). An export without `.git`, such as `git archive`, fails by design, because the API stage reads the tracked files from the context's git index: shown on the 13:11Z run's tree ("Earlier runs") and not repeated on the run tree, whose Dockerfile is unchanged since that tree. Because every stage copies manifests by name and reads sources only through read-only bind mounts, copying out of `src/` only what git tracks, host build output and untracked or ignored files reach no layer; "Host build output does not reach the image" checks this on the run tree.
- **Build network on the validation host, diagnosed on tree `b71d2dba`, 2026-11-26**: the Docker bridge network (MTU 1500, over a 1460-byte uplink) could not complete TLS to `api.nuget.org`. `dotnet restore` stopped with `NU1301` after 10 minutes, and `curl` from a bridge container timed out, while the same request with `--network host` returned 200. The builds were therefore run as `docker build --network host …`; the Dockerfile needs no change for this. The bridge-network builds of `d450f367` and the 13:11Z run's tree ("Earlier runs") and of the run tree (above) met the same `NU1301` timeout.

- Relate fields and child forms (P2): not delivered. Neither seeded form has a relate field or a child form (A7), so the feature can be neither built against nor proved without changing `testdata/`; relate stays `unsupported`. See [Undelivered items](#undelivered-items).
- SQLite `IDataStore` (P2): not delivered. The repository holds no SQLite copy of `EpiRehearsal`, so the store would have nothing to run or be proved against; the run tree holds no file, package or registration for it. See [Undelivered items](#undelivered-items).
- Parity matrix (P2): not delivered. Its statuses can only be stated against delivered, tested code, and P0 does not all pass on the run tree: SC-1, SC-5, SC-6 and SC-8 fail ("Success criteria"). See [Undelivered items](#undelivered-items).
- Delivered, each with its proof from this run on the run tree:
  - Interpreter (P1): `tests/EpiInfo.CheckCode.Tests`, 1,026 tests, 1,026 passed.
  - Field-event behaviour (P1): `FieldEventEndpointTests` 72 passed and `CheckCodeScenarioTests` 25 passed, none skipped, because `PendingP1Ids` is empty.
  - Client events (P1): `FieldEffects.test.tsx` 14 passed, `fieldEventQueue.test.ts` 57 passed, and the smoke test's step "SC-10: check code assigns Age when DOB is left" passed in `npm run e2e`.
  - Permission awareness (P2): `SqlPermissionReaderTests` 14 passed and `MissingPagePermissionSqlTests` (renamed `WritePermissionSqlTests` after the run) 1 passed on the seeded login, `PermissionTests` 37 passed with denials through `FakeDataStore`, and `FormView.test.tsx` 156 passed, its permission case "enables Save, Delete and New by the flags" among them. On the API, the form definitions of both forms carry `canInsert`, `canUpdate` and `canDelete` true for the seeded login. The proof with a real denied principal is not made; it is recorded under [Parity gaps](#parity-gaps) and in "Proofs not made".
  - Command-line switch mapping (P2): `web/src/App.tsx` reads `?view=<form name>&record=<1-based position>` on load, matching the name exactly and then ignoring case, ignores `title` and `project` (legacy `Epi.Windows.Enter/EntryPoint.cs:24-27`), and rewrites the query string in place as the open record changes. `record` counts the set the "Show deleted" toggle selects, so the shell writes `&deleted=1` while the toggle is on and drops it when the toggle is off; on load, `deleted=1` or `true` (trimmed, any case) opens the form with the toggle on, and any other value leaves it off. A URL that names no form, such as `/?record=3` or `/?view=&record=2`, shows the forms list and is rewritten to `/`. A `record` that cannot be opened as asked opens the first record (a value that is not a record number), the last record (a position past the end, after "This record no longer exists."), or New on an empty set, with a dismissible notice that names the value as written and says what opened instead. The forms-list notices, "Form '<name>' was not found." and "This form no longer exists.", have a Dismiss button too. The `deleted` switch, the rewrite to `/`, the notices that name a `record` as written and the Dismiss buttons came after the run, and "Changed after the run" gives their checks. In the run, `App.test.tsx` 27 passed, among them the case that opens record 2 of `CaseReport` from the user example `/?view=CaseReport&record=2`.
  - SC-11 performance (P2): `PerformanceTests` 2 tests, 2 passed.
  - Docker image (P2): the [Docker check](#docker-check): from a clean git clone of the run tree, the no-cache build, the image's configuration, the default listener, the ports-default rejection, the three requests with `--network host`, the save stamp and the host-output check pass. The build on the host's default bridge network fails on `NU1301` from that network, while the same build with `--network host` passes; neither it nor the by-design failure of a build from a `git archive` export, which lacks the `.git/index` the file's header requires (shown on the 13:11Z run's tree, "Earlier runs"), is a defect of the Dockerfile.
  - Higher test coverage (P2): delivered as additional test cases in the existing test files; the plan adds no coverage tool, so no coverage figure is reported.

## Boundary checks

### Legacy `Tests/` versus new `tests/` (A14)

Every check below was repeated on 2026-11-26 on the run tree, and each gave the result shown: the `git` checks in a checkout of it, and the MSBuild evaluations in a `git archive` export of it, so that no evaluation ran inside a checkout.

- **`git config core.ignorecase`**: unset (exit status 1).
- **File system**: ext4 on Linux, case-sensitive, so `Tests/` and `tests/` are two directories.
- **`git ls-files tests Tests`**: 61 paths, the four legacy files under `Tests/` and 57 files under `tests/`. The four legacy files and the four test projects are:

  ```text
  Tests/CreateMapImage.cs
  Tests/Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs
  Tests/Tests.csproj
  Tests/packages.config
  tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests/EpiInfo.Api.Tests.csproj
  tests/EpiInfo.CheckCode.Tests/EpiInfo.CheckCode.Tests.csproj
  tests/EpiInfo.Data.Tests/EpiInfo.Data.Tests.csproj
  tests/EpiInfo.Domain.Tests/EpiInfo.Domain.Tests.csproj
  ```

- **Collision**: none. No two of the 61 paths are equal once lower-cased, and no file under `tests/` has the name of one of the four legacy files, so a case-insensitive checkout that merges the two folders also has no same-name file.
- **`tests/Directory.Build.props` and `Tests/Tests.csproj`**: on ext4, with the two folders laid out as the commit holds them, MSBuild's upward search from `Tests/` never reaches `tests/`, so the props file is not imported at all: `dotnet msbuild Tests/Tests.csproj -getProperty:DirectoryBuildPropsPath -getProperty:Nullable -getProperty:TargetFramework -getProperty:UsingMicrosoftNETSdk` exits 0 and reports all four empty. Neither file changed between `d450f367` and the run tree.
- **Guard check for a case-insensitive checkout** (pass, SDK 10.0.115, evaluation only, legacy project never built or edited; first run once the props file was added, then repeated on copies of both files taken from the run tree). A copy of `Tests/Tests.csproj` was evaluated beside a copy of the props file, the layout a merged `Tests/` and `tests/` folder gives:
  - `-preprocess` shows the props file imported, through the `Microsoft.Common.props` import that `Microsoft.CSharp.targets` makes for old-style projects;
  - `UsingMicrosoftNETSdk` is empty, and `TargetFramework`, `Nullable`, `WarningsAsErrors`, `ImplicitUsings`, `IsPackable`, `IsTestProject` and the `Using` items are all empty, while `TargetFrameworkVersion` stays `v4.6.1`;
  - a control copy of the props file with both conditions removed sets every one of them, so the `'$(UsingMicrosoftNETSdk)' == 'true'` guard is what keeps the legacy project unaffected;
  - the four SDK-style test projects evaluate `UsingMicrosoftNETSdk` as `true` and take every setting.
- **Outcome**: verified for this build machine.

### `src/`, `web/` and `docs/` (C-11, A19)

- `git ls-tree --name-only 092e3d63 src web tests` is empty: none of the three folders existed before the run, so none holds legacy content.
- At the baseline `docs/` held `Home.md`, `Home_EI7Grid.png` and `Rates.docx`, which are legacy content. They are left unchanged; the only file the run adds to `docs/` is `docs/parity-notes.md`. On the run tree and on the delivered tree, 2026-11-26, `git diff --name-status 092e3d63 HEAD -- docs` lists only `A docs/parity-notes.md` ("Tree checks").
- **Outcome**: verified.

### Enter-caller inventory (A18)

**Search rule.** Files `*.csproj`, `*.cs`, `*.config`, `*.mnu*` and `*.vdproj` outside `Epi.Windows.Enter`, `Epi.Enter.CheckCodeEngine` and `Epi.Core.EnterInterpreter`, searched for `Epi.Windows.Enter`, `Enter.exe`, `EnterWindowsModule`, `EnterMainForm`, `IEnterCheckCode` and `EnterCheckCodeEngine`:

```text
git grep -n -I -E 'Epi\.Windows\.Enter|Enter\.exe|EnterWindowsModule|EnterMainForm|IEnterCheckCode|EnterCheckCodeEngine' -- '*.csproj' '*.cs' '*.config' '*.mnu*' '*.vdproj' ':!Epi.Windows.Enter/**' ':!Epi.Enter.CheckCodeEngine/**' ':!Epi.Core.EnterInterpreter/**'
```

The search ran on the clean checkout and returned 30 lines, classified below. Repeated on 2026-11-26 on the run tree (`git grep` with the same pattern and pathspecs, run on its commit), it returns 44 lines: the same 30 lines, identical to the search at the baseline `092e3d63` because no legacy file changed, plus 14 textual hits in the run's own files, in the last row. On the 13:11Z run's tree it returned the same 44, with the `RecordSqlBuilder.cs` comment then at line 1017; at `d450f367` it returned 42, and the two added lines are in `ForbiddenReferenceTests.cs`. The last row gives each line as it stands in the tree that holds this file, where the same search, run on 2026-11-27, returns 45: the same 44 plus the `RecordQueryRules.cs` value-bound remark at line 109, which the search workload bounds change added. ## Assumptions

"Direct SQL" is a query run on the rehearsal database at write-up, 2026-11-25. It confirms the seeded data the assumption names, but not the behaviour of the new code, which only the named test proves. "Final run" is the final-tree run of the run tree on 2026-11-26 ("Run results"), on a rehearsal database freshly loaded by `db-init` through `testdata/init.sh` and checked by `99_verify` (A1); where an outcome cites it, the count is the named test class's passed count in that run's first `dotnet test EpiInfo.Web.sln`, and the cases named are the ones that carry the check. `verified` means the run-time check passed in that run and agrees with the static finding, including where that finding is that the assumption does not hold. A static finding is a planning-time reading and verifies nothing on its own.

| Id | Assumption and settling source | Run-time check | Static finding (planning time) | Outcome | Evidence; decision |
|----|--------------------------------|----------------|--------------------------------|---------|--------------------|
| A1 | The seeded forms and `testdata/` scripts load and verify, every `99_verify` row `ok = 1` (`testdata/sql/99_verify.sql:4-12`) | Database precondition | Scripts present; not runnable at planning time | verified | Final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: straight after `db-init` loaded the database through `testdata/init.sh`, at 14:32:19Z, `99_verify` returned exactly the nine named rows, each `ok = 1`, and no other row ("Database precondition"). At write-up, 2026-11-25T10:32:38Z, the same nine rows also held `ok = 1` |
| A2 | Legacy navigation orders by `UniqueKey` and excludes `RECSTATUS=0` (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1422-1878`, `Epi.Enter.CheckCodeEngine/RunTimeView.cs:673-694`, `Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:336-340`) | `NavigationTests`: the live distinct `RecStatus` values are a subset of {0, 1} | Ordering by `UniqueKey` holds. Exclusion does not: legacy counts and steps through deleted records and shows them disabled | verified | Final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: `NavigationTests` 20 passed, among them `RecStatusValues_AreSubsetOfZeroAndOne` (on both forms the live distinct values are 0 and 1, each present) and `At_StartMiddleEnd_ActiveSet` and `At_StartMiddleEnd_AllRows` (records in `UniqueKey` order). | A5 | Soft delete sets `RECSTATUS=0` on the base row only and leaves page rows in place (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:641-722`, `Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1127-1220`) | `StoredDataContractTests` asserts the target invariant | Legacy does more: where the stamp columns exist, it writes the base status three times. First it captures the on-screen values, sets `RecStatus` to 0 and saves (`GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:643-648`): one base `UPDATE` writes the last-save stamps with that 0, then every page row is rewritten (`CollectedDataProvider.cs:1145-1150, 1169-1218`). The cascade then starts with the record itself: it reloads it, sets `RecStatus` to 0 and saves it again, a second base `UPDATE` with new last-save stamps and a second rewrite of every page row. Only then does it reach related-form records: it saves each child-view record whose `FKEY` matches the same way and sets grid rows to 0 (`GuiMediator.Events.Canvas.cs:397-447`). Last, the handler sets `RecStatus` to 0 a third time on the base row, recursing into grid tables and child-view rows (`GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:649-661, 685-722`). Page rows remain | verified | Final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: `StoredDataContractTests` 3 passed. `CaseReport_create_update_delete_match_the_stored_data_contract` and `FoodHistory_create_update_delete_match_the_stored_data_contract` assert by direct SQL that `DELETE` changes only the base row's `RecStatus`, from 1 to 0, and its last-save pair, with the new `LastSaveTime`; that every page row is left as it was; that no other row of the five data tables changes; and that a repeated `DELETE` writes nothing. Decision C-6 ("Request-mandated differences") |

## Field types found

Counts come from direct SQL on `metaFields` ⋈ `metaFieldTypes` (page-bound rows) and `sys.columns` ⋈ `sys.types`; the data-field counts and column types match `testdata/forms.json`. The `fieldType` column is the target mapping. The final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26, re-confirmed both: `SqlMetadataReaderTests` 32 passed, among them `FieldTypes_PerFormMatchSeed` and `ColumnFamilies_MatchCatalogue`; `FieldTypeMapperTests` 198 passed, among them `Map_EveryCaseReportFieldOfFormsJson_IsSupportedTextNumberOrDate` and `Map_EveryFoodHistoryFieldOfFormsJson_LeavesOnlyTheSevenYesNoFieldsUnsupported`; and `FormDefinitionTests` 36 passed, among them `FieldTypesFollowTheSeededTypeNames`, which checks each form definition's `fieldType` against the seeded type name.

### Type names (C-9)

The mapper matches the seeded `metaFieldTypes` names (`testdata/sql/02_reference_data.sql:metaFieldTypes`), after lower-casing and removing spaces: `Label/Title`, `Text` and `Legal Values`, where the request says "Label", "Single-line text" and "Legal-values dropdown". All 28 seeded names were read; the ones in use are those in the two tables above.

### Column families

| Table | Column types (count) |
|-------|----------------------|
| `CaseReport`, `FoodHistory` (base) | `int` 1 (`UniqueKey`), `smallint` 1 (`RecStatus`), `nvarchar(255)` 4 (`GlobalRecordId`, `FKEY`, `FirstSaveLogonName`, `LastSaveLogonName`), `datetime` 2 (`FirstSaveTime`, `LastSaveTime`) |
| `CaseReport1` | `nvarchar(4000)` 16, `nvarchar(255)` 19 (18 + `GlobalRecordId`), `nvarchar(20)` 3, `float` 2, `datetime` 4 |
| `FoodHistory2` | `nvarchar(4000)` 6, `nvarchar(255)` 4 (3 + `GlobalRecordId`), `nvarchar(20)` 1, `ntext` 1, `float` 6, `datetime` 6, `bit` 17, `tinyint` 5 |
| `FoodHistory3` | `nvarchar(4000)` 2, `nvarchar(255)` 1 (`GlobalRecordId`), `ntext` 1, `datetime` 1, `bit` 23, `tinyint` 2 |

- **Families seen**: `bit`, `datetime`, `float`, `int`, `ntext`, `nvarchar(n)` (20, 255, 4000), `smallint` and `tinyint`; `int` and `smallint` occur only in the base tables.
- **Column-type mismatches**: none found by direct SQL. Every supported field's column is in its admitted set, and Yes/No falls to `unsupported` under the non-character rule (C-2), not as a mismatch. In the final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26, `SqlMetadataReaderTests` 32 passed, among them `ColumnFamilies_MatchCatalogue`, which finds no mismatch either.
- **Option index range**: an Option field is admitted on `tinyint`, `smallint` or `int` with every item of `metaFields.List` at its 0-based index, whatever the item count. A submitted index the column cannot store, such as 256 on `tinyint` for the 257th item, is a field-level 400 from the validator's storage-range check, never a storage exception; no seeded field is an Option (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Metadata/FieldTypeMapper.cs`, `Map` and `IsAdmitted`). Proof, final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26, on synthetic metadata: `FieldTypeMapperTests.Map_OptionOnAdmittedColumn_StaysOptionWithEveryItemWhateverItsCount` (256 and 257 items on `tinyint`, 32,768 and 32,769 on `smallint`) and `RecordValidatorTests.ValidateInput_UnwritableOptionIndexOnTinyint_IsOneFieldError` passed.
- **Repeated field names**: `metaFields` does not make `Name` unique (`testdata/sql/01_metadata_schema.sql:145-149`), and a later page-bound row of a value-holding type that repeats a data field's name exactly is `unsupported`, with its `metaFieldTypes` name; see "Repeated field names" in "Parity gaps".
- **Fixed-width text (`nchar(n)`)**: no seeded column is `nchar`; the seeded character columns are `nvarchar(n)` and `ntext` only (table above). A text, multiline or legal-values field is admitted on an `nchar(n)` column, where a value is representable at up to n UTF-16 code units, as on `nvarchar(n)`, judged as given with trailing spaces counted and never trimmed. Longer text is a field-level 400, `The value is longer than the <n> characters this field can store.`, with nothing written. Text is bound as given, a logon-name stamp on an `nchar(n)` stamp column included, and SQL Server pads shorter text with spaces to n. Stored values are returned exactly as stored, padding included, and never trimmed; code-table options are the stored texts verbatim, padded when the code table is `nchar`; and legal-values membership stays ordinal. A short value therefore reads back padded, and a short legal choice reads back as a padded text that is no option (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Metadata/FieldTypeMapper.cs`, `IsRepresentable`; `src/EpiInfo.Data/Sql/ColumnValueConverter.cs`; `src/EpiInfo.Data/Metadata/SqlMetadataReader.cs`, `ToOptions`). See "Fixed-width text columns (nchar)" in "Parity gaps". Proof, on synthetic metadata and session `#` tables: `FieldTypeMapperTests.IsRepresentable_TextOnBoundedCharacterColumn_IsAtMostTheColumnsCodeUnits`; `RecordValidatorTests.ValidateInput_TextUpToTheNcharWidth_IsAcceptedAsSubmitted`, `ValidateInput_TextLongerThanTheNcharWidth_IsTooLongForTheColumn` and `ValidateInput_PaddedReadBackOfAShortNcharOption_IsNotALegalValue`; `StoredValueConversionTests` (`tests/EpiInfo.Data.Tests/Records/SqlRecordRepositoryTests.cs`) `FromDbValue_TextOnNcharColumn_IsTheStoredTextWithItsPadding`, `ToParameter_TextUpToTheNcharWidth_IsBoundUnchanged` and `ToParameter_TextLongerThanTheNcharWidth_IsRefusedBeforeBinding`; and `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` `FixedWidthColumns_ShortValues_AreStoredPaddedAndReadBackAsStored`, `FixedWidthColumns_TextsDifferingOnlyInTrailingSpaces_StoreOneValue`, `FixedWidthColumns_FullWidthChoiceEndingInSpaces_RoundTripsExactly`, `FixedWidthColumns_ShortValueWrittenByDirectSql_ReadsBackAsStoredPaddingIncluded` and `FixedWidthStampColumns_ShortLogonName_IsStoredPaddedByInsertUpdateAndSoftDelete`. They were added after the run, and their results are those "Changed after the run" gives.

### Patterns and other field rules

| Pattern | Fields | Server rule |
|---------|--------|-------------|
| `###-###-####` | `CaseReport` `PhoneNumber`, `PhysicianPhone`, `PersonCompletingFormPhone` | Text `#` mask, a pattern with at least one `#` and no letter: exact length, a digit at each `#`, and every other character, a digit included, as a literal |
| `###` | `CaseReport` `Age`, `AntibioticUseDays` | Number mask: at most three integer digits, no decimals; a leading sign is allowed and does not count as an integer digit |
| `###.#` | `FoodHistory` `FeverTemp` | Number mask: at most three integer digits and one decimal digit; a leading sign is allowed and does not count as an integer digit |
| `MM-DD-YYYY` | 11 date fields | None: the ISO wire value `YYYY-MM-DD` is not checked against it. On the client it formats only the disabled display: `web/src/components/fields/DateInput.tsx` shows an enabled date field as a native date control whose value is the ISO string, shown in the browser's locale, with `min` and `max` from the ISO bounds, and a disabled one as read-only text through the mask (2010-02-06 as `02-06-2010`), so the plan's "display and entry format for the client" (0.3.4.5) is met for that display only; see "Date display and entry mask" in "Parity gaps" |

- **Other patterns**: none found, so no field is left with "no server check". The `FoodHistory` phone field has no pattern.
- **Ranges** (`Lower`/`Upper`): `CaseReport` `Age` 0–120 and three date ranges; `FoodHistory` `FeverTemp` 80–110 and six date ranges such as `01-15-2011`–`12-31-2012`.
- **`MaxLength`**: `Zip` = 5, the only field with a value above 0.
- **Read-only**: `StateIDNumber`, `CDCIDNumber`, `ReportedToCDCDate` and `OnsetWeek`.
- **Required**: no field has `IsRequired = 1` (direct SQL: 0 of the 155 `metaFields` rows). The required gate is therefore proved with synthetic metadata through `FakeDataStore` (SC-6's required-gate proof; SC-6 itself fails on its recorded P0 gaps, "Run results"), status of that proof `pass`: in the final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26, `RequiredGateTests`, in `ValidationTests.cs`, 59 passed, among them `PostMissingEveryRequiredFieldListsEachOne` and `PutWhoseOverlayLacksAStoredRequiredValueIsRejected`, and `ValidationTests` 52 passed on the seeded forms. A required read-only field is gated too (C-3).

### SC-8 reconciliation (C-7)

| Measure | `CaseReport` | `FoodHistory` |
|---------|--------------|---------------|
| `metaFields` rows | 64 | 91 |
| System rows with a null `PageId` (`UniqueKey`, `RecStatus`, `GlobalRecordId`), not listed | 3 | 3 |
| Page-bound rows listed in the form definition | 61 (`Page 1`: 61) | 88 (`Page 1`: 55, `Page 2`: 33) |
| Page-table data columns, the request's 43/74 | 43 | 74 |
| DTO data fields | 43 | 67 (74 minus the seven Yes/No fields) |

`metaFields.ControlFontSize` is created as `decimal(2)`, so 8.25 is stored as 8 (`testdata/README.md`, finding 5). That legacy schema quirk is left as it is, and the new application does not read the column.


## Request-mandated differences

Each entry states the decided target behaviour and its legacy source, and names the test that proves the delivered code follows it. Every test named here is in the run tree and passed in its final run, 2026-11-26: `TransactionalSaveTests` 1, `RecordWriteOutcomeTests` 22, `StoreFailureWriteOutcomeTests` 42, `ErrorContractTests` 384, `SqlErrorClassifierTests` 166, `ValidationTests` 52, `RequiredGateTests` 59, `RecordValidatorTests` 324, `NavigationTests` 20, `ListAndSearchTests` 26, `StoredDataContractTests` 3, `ReadEquivalenceTests` 1, `SqlPermissionReaderTests` 14, `PermissionTests` 37, `MissingPagePermissionSqlTests` (renamed `WritePermissionSqlTests` after the run) 1, `SqlIdentifierTests` 26, `IdentifierProvenanceTests` 17, `RecordCommandIdentifierProvenanceTests` 2, `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` 56 and `EpiWeekFunctionTests` 87 passed under `dotnet test`, and `fieldEventQueue.test.ts` 57, `useRecordEditor.test.ts` 120 and `FormView.test.tsx` 156 passed under `npm test`.

### Intentional behaviour changes

These are the only two departures the request designates as intentional changes.

| Change | Legacy | Target | Owner; proof |
|--------|--------|--------|--------------|
| Transactional save | Base and page statements run with no transaction (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:927-1392`) | Insert, update and soft delete each run in one `SqlTransaction` at `READ COMMITTED`, so no partial record is ever committed. An exception before the commit call triggers one rollback attempt, whose own failure is logged and never replaces the original classification. The 500 or 503 of a failed write carries `writeOutcome`, which says what is known about the data and never changes the status. It is `notWritten` when no DML was sent, the rollback completed without error, or the server reported the transaction aborted as deadlock victim (1205). DML counts as sent only once an `INSERT` or `UPDATE` is dispatched (`src/EpiInfo.Data/Records/SqlRecordRepository.cs`, `WriteScope.MarkDmlSent`), so a write that fails before its first one, such as in the locked read, the page-row read or the in-transaction permission read, is `notWritten`, and so is an update that dispatched none and then fails to commit. A failure while the response of a store result that wrote nothing is written, the 400, 403, 404 or 409 of a `PUT`, or the 404 or already-deleted 204 of a `DELETE`, is `notWritten` too. It is `unknown` when DML was sent and neither a commit nor a completed rollback can be confirmed: the commit call failed or did not return, or the rollback itself failed. It is `written` when the store reported the commit and a later step failed before the response started, such as serialising the response, or when releasing the committed transaction or its connection failed (`ReleaseCommittedAsync`). The handler takes the request's record first: `written` once the store reported a commit, `notWritten` once it reported a result that wrote nothing or while no store write call was issued, and otherwise the store exception's own outcome, else `unknown` (`src/EpiInfo.Api/Errors/WriteOutcomeFeature.cs`; `src/EpiInfo.Api/Errors/ApiExceptionHandler.cs`, `ResolveWriteOutcome`; `src/EpiInfo.Data/Sql/SqlErrorClassifier.cs`, `ResolveWriteOutcome`). A failure after the response started aborts it, so the client has no confirmed response and treats the write as possibly applied. The post-commit log line never produces `written`: `RecordService.LogWrite` swallows a log provider's failure, so the committed response stands (`src/EpiInfo.Api/Services/RecordService.cs`). The client resends nothing on its own (`web/src/state/useRecordEditor.ts`, `saveFailureBanner`, `deleteFailureBanner`, `retry` and `saveAgain`; `web/src/api/errors.ts`, `isSafeToResend`). A `POST` (insert) or `PUT` (update) that fails with a 500, a 503 or no response offers Retry, a user action, only when its `writeOutcome` is `notWritten` and no earlier write of the same draft may have been applied. After `unknown`, `written`, a missing or unrecognised `writeOutcome`, no response, or a success response whose body does not acknowledge the write, the banner keeps the draft, says that the save may already have been applied and that the record list cannot prove whether it was, and offers only "Save again", an explicit action whose accessible name names the risk: "Save again (this may create a duplicate record)" for a `POST`, whose banner also says so, and "Save again (the earlier save may already have been applied)" for a `PUT`. Until a write of the draft succeeds, the record proves deleted (409) or the draft is replaced, saving, leaving the record and Retry are refused and the banner is shown again; a Save again's consent covers only the one request it sends. A `DELETE` (soft delete) that fails with a 500, a 503 or no response offers Retry whatever its `writeOutcome`, because a `DELETE` of an already-deleted record returns 204 and writes nothing | `src/EpiInfo.Data/Records/SqlRecordRepository.cs`. Proofs, final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: `TransactionalSaveTests` (SC-7) 1, `RecordWriteOutcomeTests` 22, `StoreFailureWriteOutcomeTests` 42, `ErrorContractTests` 384 (among them `WriteFailureCarriesTheOutcomeTheStoreReported` and `ResponseSerializationFailureAfterCommitIsUnexpectedAndWritten`), `SqlErrorClassifierTests` 166 (among them `ResolveWriteOutcome_StageAndEvidence_GiveTheTableOutcome`) and `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` 56 (among them `UpdateDispatchingNoDml_FailingWithAnUnconfirmedRollback_IsNotWritten` and `WriteDispatchingDml_FailingWithAnUnconfirmedRollback_IsUnknown`) passed under `dotnet test`, and `useRecordEditor.test.ts` 120 under `npm test` |
| Command timeout | 1,500 s (`Epi.Data.SqlServer/SqlDatabase.cs:103`; `Epi.Core/Data/DBReadExecute.cs:585, 655, 755`) | `Database:CommandTimeoutSeconds`, default 30 s, settable as `Database__CommandTimeoutSeconds` | `src/EpiInfo.Data/DatabaseOptions.cs`, `src/EpiInfo.Api/appsettings.json` |

### Request requirements

- **C-2: Yes/No fields are never written.**
  - Legacy: the seven Yes/No fields use `tinyint` columns in both page tables (`Epi.Core/Services/Fields/YesNoField.cs:95-96, 192-195`; `Epi.Data.SqlServer/SqlDatabase.cs:316-317`): `ILL`, `DoctorVisit`, `Hospitalized`, `Antibiotics` and `Died` in `FoodHistory2` (`testdata/sql/05_data_tables.sql:104, 112-114, 117`), and `StoolSubmitted` and `O157H7isolated` in `FoodHistory3` (`testdata/sql/05_data_tables.sql:149-150`). Enter writes them. A selected Yes is stored as 1 and a selected No as 0 (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.cs:1888-1902`). No selection is stored as `NULL` (`GuiMediator.cs:1925-1927`; `Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1079-1085, 1293-1299`). The seeded rows hold only 0, 1 and `NULL`, with 761 `NULL` cells across the seven columns (direct SQL at write-up).
  - Target: a seeded type outside both type lists maps to `unsupported` unless its column is character, so Yes/No renders as a disabled placeholder naming the type, is absent from `values`, is never written and is not searchable. An insert leaves those columns `NULL`, and an update leaves them unchanged. The request's "plain text input, saved as text" applies to the character-column types Phone Number and Comment Legal, which map to `text`.
- **C-3: Read-only fields are never written from the client.**
  - Legacy: check-code `ASSIGN` results are saved into read-only fields. Seeded `OnsetWeek` is read-only and assigned by `ASSIGN OnsetWeek = EPIWEEK(OnsetDate)` (`FoodHistory` check code line 18, `testdata/sql/03_forms.sql:72`).
  - Target: a read-only field present in `values` is a 400. The client never sends read-only fields (`StateIDNumber`, `CDCIDNumber`, `ReportedToCDCDate`, `OnsetWeek`); an assigned read-only value is shown on screen and not saved. Proof: `ValidationTests`, `fieldEventQueue.test.ts`.
  - Consequence for a required read-only field: the required gate takes every required data field, read-only ones included ("Every missing required field is reported" in "Design reconciliations"), and no request can supply a read-only field's value, not even an `ASSIGN` result, which is shown and never saved. On a `POST` such a field is therefore always listed as missing once the record passes the empty-record rule (C-10), so no record of a form holding one can be created through the API. On a `PUT` its stored value decides: a stored non-empty value passes, and a stored `NULL` or empty text is listed as missing. Legacy, which saves `ASSIGN` results into read-only fields, could fill it from check code. The difference follows from the request's read-only rule, so it is recorded here, with C-3, and not as a parity gap. No seeded field is required, so neither seeded form reaches it. Proof, final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: `RecordValidatorTests` 324 passed, among them `ValidateInput_RequiredReadOnlyFieldPresent_IsRejected` (presence alone is the 400, an explicit `null` included), `CheckNewRecord_RequiredReadOnlyField_IsAlwaysMissing` and `CheckRequired_RequiredReadOnlyField_IsDecidedByItsStoredValue`.
- **C-5: Deleted records are hidden by default.**
  - Legacy: count, first, last, next and previous apply no `RecStatus` filter, so deleted records are navigated and shown disabled (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1422-1878`; `Epi.Enter.CheckCodeEngine/RunTimeView.cs:673-694`; `Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:336-340`).
  - Target: `count`, `at`, positions, the list and search use the active set. `includeDeleted=true` reproduces the legacy set for navigation only: `count`, `at`, positions and the navigator. Find differs, because legacy Find keeps only `RecStatus > 0` rows while search with `includeDeleted=true` also returns deleted rows (C-16; "Search semantics"). Proof: `NavigationTests`, `ListAndSearchTests`.
- **C-6: Writes around navigation and delete.**
  - Legacy: a dirty record is saved before leaving it (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:237-247`) and saved again after the next record opens (`:272-285`). Where the stamp columns exist, delete writes the base status three times. It captures the on-screen values, sets `RecStatus` to 0 and saves: one base `UPDATE` writes the last-save stamps with that 0, then every page row is rewritten. The cascade's reload and save of the record itself repeats both, the second status write, before it reaches related-form and grid rows. The handler then sets `RecStatus` to 0 a third time on the base row and on grid and child-view rows (`:641-663, 685-722`; `Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Events.Canvas.cs:397-447`; `Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1145-1150, 1169-1218`). Undelete is offered (`:665-683`), and view, page and record events run.
  - Target: save-before-leave is kept, and a 400 keeps the user on the record. The re-save after open is dropped, so a read never writes (`DATA-01`). Delete is confirmed first and discards unsaved edits; it then updates only the base row (`RecStatus = 0`, `LastSaveLogonName`, `LastSaveTime`), with no page rewrite and no cascade. A delete of an already-deleted record writes nothing. Undelete and the view, page and record events are not carried; only field events are. Proof: `StoredDataContractTests`, `ReadEquivalenceTests`, `useRecordEditor.test.ts`.
- **C-10: The empty record.**
  - Legacy: an unchecked checkbox does not make a record non-empty (`Epi.Core/View.cs:1397-1442`), and unchecked checkboxes are stored as 0.
  - Target: the API rejects a `POST` only when every submitted value is `null` once empty text becomes `null` (request-level 400, "the record has no values"). An explicit `false` is a value, so a body of only `false` checkboxes creates a record. The editor follows legacy emptiness: a new record whose only entries are untouched or unchecked checkboxes is never posted and is discarded on leaving. When it does post, it sends `false` for each unchecked writable checkbox, so stored rows hold 0 as legacy writes; read-only checkboxes are never sent. Proof: `ValidationTests`, `useRecordEditor.test.ts`.
- **C-16: One active-set predicate.**
  - Legacy: Find appends `RecStatus > 0` (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:2249`); navigation filters nothing.
  - Target: the active set is `RecStatus = 1` for `count`, `at`, positions, the list and search, and `includeDeleted=true` selects every row. Find's predicates are therefore legacy `RecStatus > 0` against target `RecStatus = 1` by default, and no status filter with `includeDeleted=true`. With the toggle off, the two differ only for values other than 0 and 1; the live database holds only 0 and 1 (A2). With the toggle on, target search also returns `RecStatus = 0` rows, and any other value at or below 0, which legacy Find excludes. Proof: `NavigationTests`.

### Design reconciliations

- **C-15: Date fields.** A `date` field is `YYYY-MM-DD` on the wire and is stored at 00:00:00 in its `datetime` column. Legacy keeps the time of day (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1302-1315`). The time-bearing types, Date/Time and Time, are unsupported and not seeded.
- **C-17: Navigator coordinates.** The Record DTO's `position` and `count` stay intrinsic in every response: over the active set when `recStatus` is 1, over all rows when it is 0. The navigator's "[n] of N" follows the deleted toggle. N is `count` with the toggle's `includeDeleted`; n is the requested `at` position, or the `position` returned by `GET .../records/{globalRecordId}/position?includeDeleted=<toggle>`, and never comes from search. Legacy numbers the open record, whose `UniqueKey` is k, by `Select Count(*) From [<base table>] Where UniqueKey <= k` with no `RecStatus` filter, so its "[n] of N" counts deleted rows too (C-5). It adds a `FKEY` filter only for a related view, and neither seeded form is one (`Epi.Enter.CheckCodeEngine/RunTimeView.cs:673-694`; the query is at `:687`). The target keeps that formula but applies it over the selected set: the active set `RecStatus = 1` (C-16), or every row with `includeDeleted=true`. With the toggle on, n and N equal legacy's. With it off, both leave out deleted rows, so an active record preceded by deleted records has a smaller n.
- **C-19: Five additive extensions**, each optional or extra, so every specified shape still holds:
  - the Field DTO's `members` (a group's field names from `metaFields.List`, `null` otherwise; C-8);
  - search `excludeGlobalRecordId`;
  - a 400 on `GET .../records/count` for an invalid `includeDeleted`;
  - the ProblemDetails `writeOutcome` member (`notWritten`, `unknown`, `written`) on write 500 and 503 responses;
  - `GET .../records/{globalRecordId}/position`, returning `{position, count}` over the selected set without changing the Record DTO (see C-17 for the legacy `UniqueKey <= k` count it replaces). It reads only the base table, so a known record whose page row is missing is still placed and never disappears. A known record outside the selected set, such as a deleted record with the toggle off, returns `position: null` with the set's `count`. When the toggle is switched off while a deleted record is open, the navigator therefore opens `at/{min(n, count)}`. An id not in the base table is a 404, an invalid `includeDeleted` a 400, an unexpected failure a 500 and an unavailable database a 503.
- **C-18: Find.** The target choices are listed under "Search semantics".
- **Delete confirmation.** Legacy asks nothing: the "Mark as Deleted" menu item raises `MarkAsDeletedRecordEvent` straight away (`Epi.Windows.Enter/Forms/EnterMainForm.cs:801-812`). The mediator hands it to `MarkAsDeletedRecordHandler` (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Event.Handlers.cs:54`), which saves the on-screen values as part of the delete (`:641-663`; C-6). A confirmation dialog, stating that unsaved changes to the record will be discarded, comes before any request. Cancel sends nothing. Confirm sends only the base-only `DELETE`, with no `PUT` before it, and a never-saved record is discarded with no request.
- **Server pattern rule.** Legacy loads the pattern into a WinForms `MaskedTextBox` as an entry aid, with no save-time check, so partial entries were accepted (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/ControlFactory.cs:379-393`). The target checks the seeded patterns on the server ("Field types found"). This is a deliberate target rule, not legacy parity. It applies only to submitted values, so stored historical values such as `(555) 0148-6367` (`testdata/sql/07_synthetic_records.sql`) are never re-validated.
- **Save stamps.**
  - Insert: legacy writes `FirstSave*` with the base row, then `LastSave*` and `RecStatus` in a separate `UpdateBaseTable` statement, each pair from its own `DateTime.Now` (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:963-1004`). The target writes one stamp, truncated to whole seconds, to both pairs.
  - Update: legacy rewrites every column of every page (`:1225-1392`). The target writes only the submitted columns, so concurrent saves of different fields both survive. There is no row-version check: the later commit wins for fields both saves submitted, as in legacy.
- **Insert on a form whose page table is missing.** Legacy `SaveRecord(Page)` returns without writing when the page table does not exist (`if (TableExists(page.TableName) == false) return 0;`, `Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1043-1048`), so the record is saved without that page's row. The target inserts a row on every page, and refuses a form one of whose pages has no table at every form metadata read, as it refuses a page table without its `GlobalRecordId` key column ("Metadata object roles"). A read, insert, update or delete of such a form therefore answers 500 at that read, before any record statement and before the permission check, and nothing is written; every write makes that read again inside its own transaction before any DML. The store keeps its own guard below that refusal, for a schema built with a page that has no table, as the reserved-word fixture builds one in memory: the insert builds the base and every page `INSERT` before it sends the first, building that page's `INSERT` fails, and the store refuses the insert before any DML: no `INSERT` or `UPDATE` is sent, the transaction rolls back with nothing written, and the response is a 500 with `writeOutcome` `notWritten`. A valid page table with no row for the record reads as `NULL` values through the `LEFT JOIN`. Neither seeded form lacks a page table. Proof: `SqlMetadataReaderObjectRoleTests.ResolveRecordTables_BindsTheBaseAndEveryPageTableOrRefusesTheForm` (a missing page table and a page table without `GlobalRecordId` each refuse the form), passed 2026-11-27, and `ReservedWordIdentifierTests.InsertOnPageWithoutCatalogueTableOrKey_IsRefusedWithNothingWritten`, whose two cases, a second page with no table and one whose table lacks the key column, passed in the final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: each insert fails with the store's `DataStoreWriteException` carrying `WriteOutcome.NotWritten`, and neither the base table nor the first page's table holds a row.
- **Every missing required field is reported.** Legacy stops at the first (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/GuiMediator.Events.ViewExplorer.cs:58-139`). The target lists each missing required field, in data-field order, with the verbatim `FIELD_IS_REQUIRED_LONG` text, `{0} is a required field (page {1}). Please fill in this field before attempting to leave the record, save data, or close the form. `, its prompt exactly as stored as `{0}`, a `null` prompt as empty text, and its page name as `{1}` (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/ValidationMessages.cs`, `FormatFieldIsRequired`). The gate takes every required data field, read-only ones included, because legacy's required check exempts no read-only field (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/RecordValidator.cs:751-756`, `FieldRule`; the remarks of `CheckRequired`, `:350-366`). A field is missing when its effective value is absent, `null` or empty text, and whitespace is a value (`AddMissingRequiredFields`, `:715-731`): on a `POST` the submitted values, on a `PUT` the stored record overlaid with them. The client's mirror leaves a required read-only field to the server, because the client can never fill one (`web/src/validation/clientValidation.ts`, `isRequiredGated` and `checkRequired`), so the server's field-level 400 is what reports it. A read-only field can never be submitted (C-3), so a required read-only field is always missing on a `POST` and is decided by its stored value on a `PUT`; that consequence is recorded under C-3 in "Request requirements". No seeded field is required ("Patterns and other field rules"). Proof, final run of the run tree, 2026-11-26: `RecordValidatorTests` 324 passed, among them `CheckNewRecord_RequiredFieldsAbsent_ListsEveryOne`, `CheckNewRecord_RequiredFieldPrompt_IsInsertedVerbatim` and `FieldIsRequiredLong_IsTheLegacyTextVerbatim`; `RequiredGateTests` 59 passed, among them `PostMissingEveryRequiredFieldListsEachOne`.
- **Permission rule (P2, delivered).** `canInsert` needs INSERT on the base and every page table, as legacy computes it. `canUpdate` needs UPDATE on the base and every page table: legacy checks page UPDATE only when `canInsertRecords` is true (`Epi.Windows.Enter/Forms/EnterMainForm.cs:1485-1500`), while the target always checks it. A separate `canDelete` needs UPDATE on the base table, because soft delete updates only the base row (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Storage/FormPermissions.cs:198-231`, `CanInsert`, `CanUpdate` and `CanDelete`). A table with no entry, such as a page table the catalogue does not hold, denies.
  - Enforcement: `RecordService` reads the permissions afresh on every write through `IDataStore.GetPermissionsAsync`, which calls the permission reader each time, and nothing is cached (`src/EpiInfo.Data/SqlServerDataStore.cs:292-297`, `src/EpiInfo.Data/Permissions/SqlPermissionReader.cs`). It applies the rule to its own read in one place, `IsAllowed` (`src/EpiInfo.Api/Services/RecordService.cs:665-675`); a store that reports no permissions means no check. `POST` needs `canInsert` (`RecordService.cs:368-372`) and `DELETE` needs `canDelete` (`:567-571`), each checked on the service's read before the write starts and passed to the store only to turn the store's own check on (`:378-379`, `:578-579`).
