That form carries a time, as the desktop's form does in every culture, so, as in legacy, a date never equals date-only text: text `"2011-01-15"` does not equal the date 2011-01-15, and neither does `"1/15/2011 12:00:00 AM"`, while `"01/15/2011 00:00:00"` does.
- **Why not equal:** the server does not know the desktop's date format, so it cannot write the date as that culture would, and the invariant culture's form is the same on every server. The ISO wire format `YYYY-MM-DD` (C-15) is not used here, because legacy never compared a date in a date-only form. No seeded program compares a date with text.

### Legal Values options

- **Legacy:** the whole code table is read without `ORDER BY`, the exact-distinct texts of `TextColumnName` are kept with `DataView.ToTable(true, ...)`, which compares values with `Equals`, and a `NULL` row shows as an empty item. With `Sort` = 1 the ComboBox sorts under the process's current culture with `CompareOptions.StringSort`, case-sensitive (`Epi.Core/Services/Fields/TableBasedDropdownField.cs:79-110`; `Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/ControlFactory.cs:506, 537-541`).
- **Kept:** the same unsorted rows, every code table of a request read in one batch; the exact-distinct texts as stored, empty and white-space texts included; with `Sort` = 1, one sort by the metadata reader under the API process's current culture with `StringSort`, whose order the form definition keeps (`src/EpiInfo.Data/Metadata/SqlMetadataReader.cs`).
- **Why not equal:** a `NULL` row is not offered, because an option's value and label are both its stored text and a `NULL` has none; leaving the field empty still saves `NULL`, as legacy saves its empty item. Texts the culture ranks equal, such as a decomposed and a precomposed `é`, are ordered ordinally, where legacy's order for them depends on the order of insertion. The seeded Legal Values fields (`FoodHistory` `Sex`, `EthnicityGroup` and `LooseStoolsPerDay`) all have `Sort` = 0, and their code tables hold no `NULL`, blank or case-variant row.

### EPIWEEK date text

- **Check code:** any `EPIWEEK` call whose first argument is text, from a Text field or a string literal. No seeded program does this: the one seeded call, `ASSIGN OnsetWeek = EPIWEEK(OnsetDate)` (`FoodHistory` check code line 18, `testdata/sql/03_forms.sql:72`), passes the Date field `OnsetDate`.
- **Legacy behaviour:** `Rule_EPIWEEK` reads any present first argument with `DateTime.TryParse` of its text in the current culture, keeping the time of day, and returns null when that fails, before it reads the first day (`Epi.Core.EnterInterpreter/Rules/Functions/Rule_EPIWEEK.cs:40-47`).
- **Behaviour kept:** text in two families is read with legacy's own lenient parser, `DateTime.TryParse`, in the invariant culture, time of day included:
  - the year-first numeric family: a year of four or more digits, a month and a day separated by `-` or `/`;
  - the named-month family: a day, a full Title-case month name other than `May` (`January` to `April`, `June` to `December`) and a year of four or more digits, as day, month and year (`2 January 2011`), month, day and year (`January 2, 2011`) or year, month and day (`2011 December 31`), with white space between the parts, or a comma and optional white space before a year that comes last, optionally after a full Title-case weekday name (`Sunday` to `Saturday`) and a comma or white space (`Saturday, December 31, 2011`).

  Either may be followed, after `T`, a comma or white space, by `H:mm` with optional `:ss`, an optional `.` or `,` fraction of any length (rounded to ticks, as legacy rounds it) and an optional upper-case `AM` or `PM`, or by an hour with `AM` or `PM`, with surrounding white space allowed. Every spelling of both families holds a year of four or more digits and a day, so no reading takes either from the clock. So `2011-12-31T10:00:00.12345678`, `2011/01/02`, `02011-01-02`, `2011-01-02 10:00 AM`, `2011-05-04 10 PM`, `2011-05-04, 10:00`, `January 2,2011`, `Sunday,January 2,2011 10:00 AM` and `February 28, 2011 13:00` are read as legacy reads them. Text of either shape the parser rejects, such as `2011-02-30`, `2011-01-01T1:05`, a `24:00` time, `February 30, 2011` or a weekday that is not the date's (`Monday, January 2, 2011`, which no culture reads), gives null, as in legacy. The text is read in the Gregorian calendar, as legacy reads it under every culture whose calendar is Gregorian; under a culture with another calendar legacy reads even `2011-01-01` differently, as 1468-01-01 under `th-TH` (Thai Buddhist) and 2632-03-21 under `fa-IR` (Persian). Every other present value, other text included, gives null as a successful call, as legacy does for text it cannot parse. A missing date stays an `error` diagnostic (`src/EpiInfo.CheckCode/Functions/EpiWeekFunction.cs`).
- **Why:** a probe ran `DateTime.TryParse` on 72,092 generated spellings under each of the 892 Gregorian-calendar cultures of .NET 10, the stand-in for the .NET Framework runtime legacy runs on: every one of the 23,624 spellings the port reads as a date was read the same in all 892 cultures and as the port reads it. Outside the families, legacy's result depends on:
  - the culture: a numeric date that is not year-first (`01/02/2011` is 1 February under 711 cultures and 2 January under 181); `.` as the date separator (`2011.01.02` fails under 15 cultures, among them `as`, `en-DK` and `en-FI`); `May`, which `bas`, `bas-CM`, `dua` and `dua-CM` read as another month (`May 5, 2011` is 5 November under `bas` and 5 October under `dua`); abbreviated month and weekday names (`Jan 2, 2011` is 2 June under `st`, `st-LS` and `st-ZA`, and `Sun, January 2, 2011` fails under `to` and `to-TO`); and other casings of the names and a lower-case `am` or `pm`, which `en-US-POSIX` does not read (`january 2, 2011`, `2011-01-02 10:00 pm`);
  - the clock: text without a year takes the current year or date: `1.5` and `1/5` are days of the current year, and `January 2` is 2 January of the current year under 334 cultures and 1 January 2002 under the other 558;
  - the server time zone: a `Z` or offset suffix, as in `2011-01-01T10:00:00+02:00`, is converted to the server's local time.

  No deterministic port can match those readings. The spellings outside the families that legacy reads alike in every culture give null by the bound of the port, not because their reading is unknown: seconds followed by a period and then white space (`2011-01-02 10:00:00.` and a tab; without the white space 105 cultures reject it), white space before a comma (`January 2 , 2011`), a month name run into the year (`2 January2011`), a year and a month without a day, alone or followed after a comma, white space or a dash by more (`2011, 10` and `2011 - 10` read as 1 October 2011, `January 2011 - 10 PM` as 10 January 2011 at noon), and an abbreviated name that collides with no culture's, such as `Tue, 1 February 2011`, because the port reads only full names. Proof: `EpiWeekFunctionTests`.

### A condition with no value

- **Legacy:** an `IF` condition and each operand of `AND` and `NOT` are read through the value's `ToString()` (`Epi.Core.EnterInterpreter/Rules/Rule_If_Then_Else_End.cs:170`; `Rule_AndExp.cs:41-44`; `Rule_NotExp.cs:40`). An empty text, phone-number or GUID field reads as empty text (`Rule_Value.cs:299-332, 400-406`), which is simply not `"true"`. Every other value-less operand is `null`: `(.)`, an empty number, date or Yes/No field, and a function that gives no value. Its `ToString()` throws, so neither branch runs and the exception leaves the whole event block, as for the boolean comparison above.
- **Kept:** the same truth, with the same exemption for an empty text-family field (`text`, `multiline`, `legalValues`), which is not true. Any other value-less operand gives one `error` diagnostic, `A condition has no value.`, with the operand's line and the statement's command. Neither branch of that `IF` runs, the effects of earlier statements stand and the next statement runs.
- **Why not equal:** a field event reports a runtime error as a diagnostic that keeps earlier effects and never blocks a save; it has no counterpart for an exception that ends the block. No seeded program tests a value-less operand: every operand of a seeded `IF`, `AND` or `NOT` is a comparison, which always has a boolean value (`IF Sex = "1"`, `IF NOT FeverTemp = (.) AND Fever = (-)`).

### Find wildcard patterns longer than 4,000 code units

- **Legacy:** the pattern is inlined as an ANSI `varchar` literal (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:2195-2206`) and runs up to 8,000 single-byte characters; a longer one fails with SQL Server error 8152. Against the `nvarchar` columns the literal is converted to `nvarchar(4000)`, so every character past the 4,000th is dropped without an error and the search answers for the shortened pattern (measured: `a` × 4,000 followed by `b` matches a stored `a` × 4,000).
- **Kept:** every pattern the bounds admit, of up to 32,767 code units ("Value bound" and "Search-wide bounds" in "Target choices"), on a text, multiline or legal-values field of either form, `CaseReport1.LastName` and the `ntext` `FoodHistory3.BuyLastTenDays` included, is matched whole with the meaning of `col LIKE @p` and every value a parameter ("Wildcard patterns" in "SQL Server limits"): within 4,000 code units as one `LIKE`, past them by the long-pattern program, which compares whole literal runs by the collation's equality and returns the rows one `LIKE` of the whole pattern returns, where the column's collation matches an expansion (`æ` and `ae` under `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS`) or ignores a character (`NUL` and, under the rehearsal database's `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`, every supplementary character; measured: `N'abc' LIKE N'%😀%'` and `N'a😀b' LIKE N'ab'` are both true there) included. A grid `q` past the limit runs as the same program's literal contains ("Grid q"). One case is a 400: a pattern holding a `[...]` set longer than 4,000 code units that has no closing `]`, or that cannot be cut into sets of at most 3,998 code units, each cut between two members where neither is `-` or a zero-width joiner, the second is neither `^` nor a combining mark and the two are not one surrogate pair, is a 400 under the field's canonical name, `<field> search patterns cannot hold a [...] set longer than 4000 characters that has no closing ] or that cannot be cut into sets of at most 3998 characters that keep its members and negation.`, after the form's metadata is read and before any Find statement is sent. And a literal run longer than a `PATINDEX` pattern, in a Find pattern or a long `q`, is sought by the leftmost hit of 33 of its prefixes, so a match is found while no character of the column's collation ranks equal to more than 33 text elements of the run: measured on SQL Server 2022, of the 11,557 BMP characters whose compatibility decomposition has three or more code units, the 314 that `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS` and the 71 that `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS` rank equal to it span at most three text elements (`ﬃ` for `ffi`), and `ﷺ` (U+FDFA) equals its 18-character decomposition under neither.
- **Why not equal:** SQL Server's `LIKE`, `PATINDEX`, `CHARINDEX` and `REPLACE` all refuse an `nvarchar` pattern past 4,000 code units (error 8152), so no single comparison takes a longer one, and converting it to a `varchar` pattern in the database code page, as legacy's literal was, is inexact: it replaces every character outside the code page, and on a double-byte code page an 8,000-byte `varchar` holds fewer than 8,000 characters, so SQL Server shortens the pattern without an error (measured: 5,000 `あ` cast under `Japanese_CI_AS` to `varchar(8000)` keep 4,000). The target therefore matches the whole pattern in steps, and does not reproduce legacy's shortening, which answered for a pattern other than the one written. A set matches one character, and SQL Server reads its `^`, its `-` and a combining mark by their neighbours in the whole set (`[a-c-e]` matches `a`, `b`, `c` and `e` but neither `-` nor `d` under `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS`, and also `-` under `Latin1_General_100_BIN2`), so a set longer than any pattern SQL Server takes is evaluated only as pieces that keep those readings. An unclosed one has no collation-independent meaning (`[a` matches `a` under `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS` and nothing under `Latin1_General_100_BIN2`), and one with no such cut, such as a chain of ranges, has no pieces, so neither runs and both are the one 400. A prefix of a literal run can end inside a value character the collation expands, which then defeats it, and no number of prefixes covers a character expanding into more text elements than that number, so the seek's 33 prefixes bound it, far past the three measured. No seeded value needs a pattern longer than 4,000 units.

### Repeated field names

- **Legacy:** `GetFields` reads a view's `metaFields` rows ordered by `ControlTopPositionPercentage`, then `ControlLeftPositionPercentage`, and adds each row's field to the view unless the view already contains a field of that name; such a row is logged as already existing, "This field has not been imported.", and left out (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/MetadataDbProvider.cs:1141-1161, 1229-1234, 1366-1386`). `Contains` compares `Name.ToLowerInvariant()`, so letter case is ignored (`Epi.Core/Collections/NamedObjectCollection.cs:154-167`). The view keeps the first row in control top-then-left position order and omits every later row whose name equals it ignoring case, exact repeats and case variants alike.
- **Kept:** the first row in storage order (page `Position`, `PageId`, `TabIndex`, `FieldId`) that holds an exact canonical name is the data field (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Metadata/FormDefinitionFactory.cs`, `DataFieldSet`, `GetDataFields`). A later page-bound row of a value-holding type that repeats that name exactly is listed on its page as `unsupported`, with its `metaFieldTypes` name as `unsupportedType`; it has no `values` entry and no validation, it is never written, and a group `List` entry naming it resolves to the data field. Reads, the grid, validation, writes, search and the Legal Values options therefore see one field. Names that differ only by letter case stay two distinct data fields with their own `values` keys, and a request name equal to both ignoring case is ambiguous and a 400 (`DataFieldSet.IsAmbiguous`; `src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/RecordValidator.cs`). The target reads no control position.
- **Why not equal:** each page of the form definition lists every `metaFields` row with its `PageId` (C-7), so a repeat is listed `unsupported` rather than omitted. The target orders fields by page `Position`, `PageId`, `TabIndex` and `FieldId` everywhere and reads no control position, so the row that holds an exact name can differ from legacy's first in control top-then-left order. Case variants, which legacy drops after the first, are kept as distinct fields, because the target's `values` keys compare ordinally. No seeded form has a repeated name, exact or ignoring case: by direct SQL, `SELECT ViewId, LOWER(Name), COUNT(*) FROM metaFields GROUP BY ViewId, LOWER(Name) HAVING COUNT(*) > 1` returns no row on the rehearsal database. Proof: `FormDefinitionFactoryTests` (`Create_RowRepeatingADataFieldNameExactly_IsListedUnsupportedAndOnlyTheFirstHoldsTheName`), `RecordValidatorTests` (`CheckNewRecord_RowRepeatingAName_GatesOnlyTheDataField`), `SqlRecordRepositoryTests` (`InsertPage_ExactNameOnTwoPages_BindsOnlyTheFirstInStorageOrder`) and a `GetOptionsAsync` repeated-name case in `SqlMetadataReaderTests`.

### Date display and entry mask

- **Fields:** the four `CaseReport` and seven `FoodHistory` date fields, each with the pattern `MM-DD-YYYY` (`testdata/sql/03_forms.sql:metaFields Pattern`; "Patterns and other field rules" in "Field types found"). The ten writable ones reach the gap while enabled; the read-only `CaseReport` `ReportedToCDCDate` is always disabled, and no seeded program disables a date field.
- **Legacy:** the pattern is not a date field's mask. `DateField` derives from `DateTimeField` and is not `IPatternable` (`Epi.Core/Services/Fields/DateField.cs:14`; `Epi.Core/Services/Fields/DateTimeField.cs:14`), so `ControlFactory` skips the `MaskedTextBox` branch that pattern-bearing number and phone fields take (`Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/ControlFactory.cs:379-393`) and gives it a plain `TextBox` (`:411-421`). Enter's own `DateTimePicker` control (`Epi.Windows.Enter/Controls/DateTimePicker.cs`) is never created. The box shows the value as `ToShortDateString()` in the desktop's current culture (`DateField.cs:169-197`), and an empty box shows that culture's short-date pattern as a watermark (`DateField.cs:146-153`; `Epi.Windows.Enter/Forms/canvas.render.cs:125-144, 151-155`). A left click opens a `MonthCalendar` (`Epi.Windows.Enter/Forms/Canvas.control.events.cs:43-92`). On leaving the box, its text is read with `DateTime.TryParse` in that culture and rewritten as its short date, or cleared when it does not parse (`:564-583`). The pattern only reads the `Lower` and `Upper` bounds (`DateField.cs:85-103, 320-347`).
- **Kept:** while the field is enabled, the ISO value on the wire and in the control, the browser's locale for display, the browser's own picker, and no mask. `DateInput` renders `<input type="date">`, whose value is the wire string `YYYY-MM-DD`; a cleared or incomplete entry yields `null`; `min` and `max` come from the ISO bounds, and the range and required checks stay the validator's. While the field is disabled, because it is protected (read-only, or on a deleted record) or disabled by check code, `DateInput` renders a read-only, disabled text input with the same id, label and ARIA state instead. It shows the value through the field's date mask when the pattern holds `YYYY`, `MM` and `DD` once each, in any case (`MM-DD-YYYY` shows 2010-02-06 as `02-06-2010`), and otherwise in the user's locale; that text is display only, the editor keeps the ISO string, and the control never emits a value (`web/src/components/fields/DateInput.tsx`, `DateInput`, `disabledDateText`). Neither the server (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/MaskPattern.cs`, `MaskPatternKind.Unchecked`) nor the client mirror (`web/src/validation/clientValidation.ts`, `interpretPattern`) checks the pattern.
- **Why not equal:** the client renders native HTML controls with no UI package (0.3.6), and a page cannot set the display format of a native date input: the browser takes it from its own locale. So for an enabled field neither the `MM-DD-YYYY` pattern nor the desktop culture's short date can be imposed, and an entry is a complete calendar date made through the control, not free text read by a culture parse. The mask governs only the disabled display. The plan's "display and entry format for the client" (0.3.4.5) is therefore met for that display and not for the enabled display or the entry, which stay unresolved; legacy did not apply the pattern to date entry either. No stored value is affected, because the wire value and the stored `datetime` at 00:00:00 (C-15) are unchanged.
- **Proof:** pass, final run, 2026-11-26, on the run tree, `npm test` in `web/`, 474 passed, among them `FormView.test.tsx` 156 and `FieldEffects.test.tsx` 14. `FormView.test.tsx`: "renders every data field as one native control labelled by its prompt", which expects a writable date field to be an `input` of type `date`, and "renders the read-only date $name, stored $stored with pattern $pattern, as a disabled read-only text box showing $shown, and writable dates as date inputs", whose seven rows cover `MM-DD-YYYY`, `DD-MM-YYYY`, `yyyy/mm/dd`, no pattern, the partial `MM-DD`, a date and time pattern, and an empty value. `FieldEffects.test.tsx`: "shows a date disabled by check code as read-only text in its date mask, and ENABLE restores its date input".

### Denied principal (P2 permissions)

- **Forms:** every form: `CaseReport` (tables `CaseReport` and `CaseReport1`) and `FoodHistory` (`FoodHistory`, `FoodHistory2` and `FoodHistory3`).
- **Legacy:** opening a form computes the permissions of the connected principal once (`Epi.Windows.Enter/Forms/EnterMainForm.cs:1429-1452`, `CheckAndSetPermissions` at `:1458-1464`), which the SQL Server driver reads with `HAS_PERMS_BY_NAME` per table (`Epi.Data.SqlServer/SqlDatabase.cs:1804-1810`). Insert needs INSERT on the base and every page table (`EnterMainForm.cs:1466-1483`); update needs UPDATE on the base table and, when insert is allowed, on every page table (`:1485-1500`).
- **Kept:** the same flags, `HAS_PERMS_BY_NAME(<table>, 'OBJECT', 'INSERT')` and `'UPDATE'` for the base and every page table, read for the connected principal on the tables the catalogue confirms, so a table it does not hold gets no entry and is denied (`src/EpiInfo.Data/Permissions/SqlPermissionReader.cs`). They are read afresh on every write and never cached. A `POST` without `canInsert`, a `PUT` without `canUpdate` and a `DELETE` without `canDelete` return 403 with nothing written (`src/EpiInfo.Api/Services/RecordService.cs`, `IsAllowed`). Every write is checked again inside its transaction, before any statement that changes data, from the permissions the repository reads on that transaction's connection: a `POST` once its insert holds the form's write lock, for the base table and every page table, refused when INSERT is denied on any of them; a `PUT` after the locked read and the required gate, for the base table and every page table, refused when UPDATE is denied on any of them or INSERT on the table of a page whose row is missing; and a `DELETE` of an active record after its locked read, for the base table alone, refused when UPDATE is denied on it. Each refusal is a 403 with nothing written, so a grant revoked while the write waited on a lock is a refusal, not a failed statement (`src/EpiInfo.Data/Records/SqlRecordRepository.cs`, `ReadPermissionsInTransactionAsync`; `SqlPermissionReader.ReadResolvedAsync`). A form whose page table, or its record key column, the catalogue lacks is refused at its metadata read, which every write repeats inside its transaction before the form's write lock and any statement on a record table, so no `INSERT` or `UPDATE` is sent and no record is committed without one of its page rows ("Insert on a form whose page table is missing" in "Design reconciliations"). The client disables New, Save and Delete by the same flags (`web/src/components/FormView.tsx`, `web/src/components/RecordNavigator.tsx`), and refuses a save started by leaving a record the same way as an explicit one: with `canInsert` `false` for a new record, or `canUpdate` `false` for a stored one, the editor sends no request and shows the permission banner, keeping the draft and offering Discard edits (`web/src/state/useRecordEditor.ts`, `runSave`). Page UPDATE is checked always, where legacy checks it only when insert is allowed ("Permission rule" in "Design reconciliations").
- **Why not equal:** the rule is ported, but a denial cannot be proved against the real database. A denied principal needs a new login or user, which the unchanged-database rule excludes, and the seeded login holds every permission on both forms, so on the rehearsal database every flag is granted and no write is denied. Denials are proved only through the fake store. This stays unresolved.
- **Proof:** pass, final run, 2026-11-26, on the run tree. `SqlPermissionReaderTests` 14 passed on the seeded login, among them `SeededLogin_GetsEveryPerTableFlag_AsHasPermsByNameReportsIt` for both forms and `MissingPageTable_GetsNoEntry_AndDeniesInsertAndUpdate`. `PermissionTests` 37 passed, with denials through `FakeDataStore`: `EachWriteFollowsTheFlagThePermissionsDerive`, `UpdateNeedsInsertOnEveryPageWhoseRowIsMissing`, `UpdateRunsTheRequiredGateBeforeTheMissingPageInsertCheck`, `TheStoreDecidesAnUpdateFromThePermissionsItHoldsWhenItRuns`, `EachWriteReadsThePermissionsAgain`, `NoReportedPermissionsMeansNoCheck`, `AnEntryInAnotherLetterCaseNeitherGrantsNorDenies` and `ResolvedTableNamesLeadEachFormNameToItsEntry`. `MissingPagePermissionSqlTests` 1 passed on the rehearsal database (`UpdatePermissions_AreReadInsideTheUpdateTransactionBeforeItsFirstWrite`): each `PUT`, on a record lacking its `FoodHistory3` row, on the update that repairs it and once every row is present, reads the permissions of all three `FoodHistory` tables inside its transaction before its first write; it shows where the check runs, not a denial. After the run, on the changed tree, 2026-11-27: `PermissionTests` 54 passed, `TheStoreDecidesEachWriteFromThePermissionsItHoldsWhenItRuns` and `AGrantRevokedAfterTheServicesReadIsRefusedByTheStore` among them, and the renamed `WritePermissionSqlTests` 3 passed, adding `InsertPermissions_AreReadInsideTheInsertTransactionAfterTheFormLockBeforeItsFirstWrite` and `DeletePermissions_AreReadInsideTheDeleteTransactionAfterTheLockedReadBeforeItsUpdate`, which show where a `POST` and a `DELETE` read their permissions inside the transaction, not a denial. The `FormView.test.tsx` permission case passed in `npm test` ("FormView permission flags", "enables Save, Delete and New by the flags: canDelete true, canUpdate false": Delete enabled and Save disabled), as did the `useRecordEditor.test.ts` case "refuses $write the flags deny without a request, and discards it in place" (an update under `canUpdate` `false` and a create under `canInsert` `false`, each refused on leaving and on Save with no request).

### Sign-only number entry

- **Fields:** every Number field a user can type into: `CaseReport` `Age` and `AntibioticUseDays`, and `FoodHistory` `CaseID`, `Age`, `Latitude`, `Longitude` and `FeverTemp`. The read-only `OnsetWeek` takes no entry.
- **Legacy:** every Number field is a `MaskedTextBox`, because `NumberField` is `IPatternable` (`Epi.Core/Services/Fields/NumberField.cs:13`; `Epi.Windows.Enter/PresentationLogic/ControlFactory.cs:379-385`). On leaving it, `control_Leave` counts the digits and the characters equal to the current culture's negative sign or decimal separator, and clears the box when it holds such a character and no digit, such as `-`, `.` or `-.` (`Epi.Windows.Enter/Forms/Canvas.control.events.cs:538-559`).
- **Kept:** the text as typed. `NumberInput`'s `interpret` turns blank text into `null` and text that reads as a finite number into that number, kept exact as a `bigint` or a decimal where a double would round it, and keeps any other text, sign-only text included, as the raw string (`web/src/components/fields/NumberInput.tsx:28-35`; `web/src/api/client.ts`, `numberFromText`, which gives no number for `-`, `.` or `-.`); its `onBlur` only raises the field event and leaves the text alone (`NumberInput.tsx:101-108`). Client validation reports the string as not a number, "Enter a number." (`web/src/validation/clientValidation.ts:31, 170-175`, `checkType`), and the save stops on that error before any request (`web/src/state/useRecordEditor.ts:1112-1116`). The server answers such text from any caller with a 400, "A finite number is expected." (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/RecordValidator.cs:547-579`, `TryCoerce`, the message at `:574`; `src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/ValidationMessages.cs:62`).
- **Why not equal:** the client plan does not port the clearing on leave. `NumberInput` keeps sign-only or separator-only text such as `-`, `.` or `-.` as typed (`NumberInput.tsx:54-59`), so the user completes or clears it where legacy cleared it. A partial entry that holds a digit is not part of this gap: `37.` keeps its typed display while its value is the number 37, which can be saved, and legacy, which clears only text with no digit, keeps it too. This stays unresolved. No stored value is affected, because such text is rejected and never written.
- **Proof:** no test pins it. `RecordValidatorTests` (`ValidateInput_NumberGivenNonNumber_IsNumberExpected`) pins the server's answer for other text on a number field (`abc`, `98`), not for sign-only text.

### Fixed-width text columns (nchar)

- **Fields:** none seeded. Every seeded character column is `nvarchar(n)` or `ntext` ("Column families" in "Field types found"), so the gap is reached only by a text, multiline or legal-values field whose page column is `nchar(n)`.
- **Legacy:** the text is written as given, as a parameter, and only empty text becomes `NULL` (`Epi.Core/Data/Services/CollectedDataProvider.cs:1293-1320`), so SQL Server stores shorter text padded with spaces to n; the record load takes the stored value as it is, padding included (`dataRow[...]`, `:1737`). Two entries that differ only in trailing spaces therefore store one value, and a short legal choice comes back as a padded text.
- **Kept:** the legacy behaviour. Writes: text of up to n UTF-16 code units is admitted on `nchar(n)`, as on `nvarchar(n)`, and longer text is a field-level 400, `The value is longer than the <n> characters this field can store.`, with nothing written (`src/EpiInfo.Domain/Metadata/FieldTypeMapper.cs`, `IsRepresentable`; `src/EpiInfo.Domain/Validation/RecordValidator.cs`). Admitted text, a logon-name stamp on an `nchar(n)` stamp column included, is bound as given, and SQL Server pads shorter text with spaces to n (`src/EpiInfo.Data/Sql/ColumnValueConverter.cs`, `ToParameter` and `ToSystemParameter`). Reads: an `nchar(n)` value is returned as stored, padding included, and never trimmed (`ColumnValueConverter.FromDbValue`), so API values equal direct SQL. Options are the code-table texts verbatim, padded when the code table is `nchar` (`src/EpiInfo.Data/Metadata/SqlMetadataReader.cs`, `ToOptions`), and a legal value must equal one of them ordinally (`RecordValidator`).
- **Why not equal:** on fixed-width storage a write and read of short text cannot round-trip exactly, because SQL Server stores it padded. Trimming on read would return values other than the stored ones, which reads must not do (`DATA-01`, SC-4), and refusing short text would break the plan's admission of up to n code units on `nchar(n)`. Two inputs that differ only in trailing spaces therefore store one value, and a saved short legal choice reads back as a padded text that is no option: resubmitting that read-back value verbatim is a 400, `The value is not one of the field's legal values.`, while choosing the option again is accepted. These stay unresolved, as in legacy.
- **Proof:** the tests named under "Fixed-width text (`nchar(n)`)" in "Column families", among them these `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` on session `#` tables: `FixedWidthColumns_ShortValues_AreStoredPaddedAndReadBackAsStored`, where a short legal choice and short text written through the repository's insert are stored padded at `DATALENGTH` 10 on `nchar(5)`, direct SQL and the repository's read return the padded texts, and the validator accepts the short choice again and refuses the padded one as not a legal value; `FixedWidthColumns_TextsDifferingOnlyInTrailingSpaces_StoreOneValue`, where `ab` and `ab ` store one value; `FixedWidthColumns_FullWidthChoiceEndingInSpaces_RoundTripsExactly`, where a full-width value ending in spaces round-trips exactly; `FixedWidthColumns_ShortValueWrittenByDirectSql_ReadsBackAsStoredPaddingIncluded`, where a short value written by direct SQL reads back padded; and `FixedWidthStampColumns_ShortLogonName_IsStoredPaddedByInsertUpdateAndSoftDelete`, where, on a base table whose logon-name stamps are `nchar(255)`, an insert, an update and a soft delete with a short logon name succeed and store it padded at `DATALENGTH` 510. Results in "Changed after the run".

| `SqlMetadataReaderTests`: column type against the admitted set | A field whose column type its `fieldType` does not admit | pass: `SqlMetadataReaderTests` 32 passed; `FieldTypes_PerFormMatchSeed` found every page-bound field of both forms mapped to its decided `fieldType`, which a column outside the admitted set would make `unsupported`, so there is no mismatch to record |
| `CheckCodeScenarioTests`: every in-scope scenario (A4) | A scenario that fails, with its id | pass: `CheckCodeScenarioTests` 25 passed, none skipped: the 24 in-scope scenarios (the 19 field events, `EC-VAL-01`..`EC-VAL-03`, `DATA-01` and `DATA-02`) and `EC-GEO-01`, so no scenario fails |
| `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` | A reserved-word round trip that cannot be proved | pass: `ReservedWordIdentifierTests` 56 passed; `ReservedWordColumns_RoundTripThroughTheRepository` proves the round trip (see "Identifier quoting"), so there is no gap to record |
| P2 permission awareness | That no real denied principal could be tested, because it needs a new login or user, which the unchanged-database rule excludes | not run: the unchanged database holds no denied principal; the gap is recorded under "Denied principal (P2 permissions)" above |
